Integration analysis · Case part 1

Chift × Horus: accounting connector mapping

For each Chift accounting route in scope: which Horus Software API endpoints implement it, what the connector has to transform, combine or emulate, and what still needs confirmation from Horus or Chift.

Prepared by Silas Deblanc For Chift, Product & Tech Date 13 October 2026 Status Analysis for discussion

1Executive summary

All eleven Chift routes in scope can be built on the Horus Software API, and none is blocked. Two map almost directly (folders and analytic plans). Nine need workarounds that the connector can absorb, at the cost of extra calls, emulated behaviour, or data loss that Chift should disclose to its clients. For most of them the core Horus capability is native; the workaround sits in the translation around it.

2
Supported
9
Supported with workaround
0
Not supported
0
To confirm
11
Chift routes analysed
40
of 72 Horus endpoints called, including optional ones

Key findings

  • Every object in scope exists in Horus and can be read or written where the case needs it, with the field-level losses listed below: third parties (“companies”), sales and purchase invoices, miscellaneous operations, accounts, daybooks, cost schemas and sections, folders and open items.
  • Creation calls return only an id. Client and account creation read the record back (one .info call) to return the full Chift response; invoices are read back by default, and miscellaneous operations only when Horus generates the number. Duplicate checks are needed before creating, because no idempotency key is documented.
  • There is no “list invoices” endpoint. Invoice retrieval rebuilds each invoice from book entries (headers) and account history lines (lines, VAT, analytics, matching), plus open-item data for payments. This is the clearest case of “one Chift route, several Horus endpoints”.
  • VAT is modelled differently. Horus has no VAT-code entity: each line carries a rate, an origin and a type (plus kind and condition for purchases), and only listed combinations are valid. The connector needs its own VAT-code catalogue to serve Chift’s tax_code and VAT-code list.
  • No documented pagination, and month-based periods. Horus lists appear to return everything for a folder or a month window, so the connector emulates Chift pagination and day-level date filters, and should cache reference data per folder.
  • Data loss Chift should disclose to its clients: one address per third party, no mobile, notes or person fields; no line quantity or unit price on invoices; the account type cannot be written; miscellaneous operations are EUR-only; analytic balances are not stored.
RecommendationProceed with a Horus connector covering all eleven routes, built on a small set of shared components: token and folder handling, a per-folder reference cache, pagination emulation, a VAT-code catalogue and an analytics converter. Before building, settle the open questions with Horus that change the design, mainly the tax-combination files, credit-note daybooks, document numbering, the currency of ledger amounts and rate limits, and get a test license, since the API works on live data.

2Scope, sources and method

What was analysed

Task A: route-level mapping

For each of the ten Chift endpoints listed in the case: the Horus endpoint(s) to use and the considerations specific to Horus.

  • Client creation
  • Invoice retrieval (multiple analytic plans)
  • Invoice creation (multiple analytic plans)
  • Journal retrieval
  • Ledger account creation
  • Chart of accounts retrieval
  • Accounting folders retrieval
  • Miscellaneous operation creation
  • Analytic plans retrieval
  • Analytic accounts retrieval (multiple plans)

Task B: attribute-level mapping

Every request attribute, response attribute and query parameter of three routes:

  • Client creation
  • Invoice creation (multiple analytic plans)
  • Outstanding items (customer and supplier balances). This route appears only in Task B, so it is also given a route-level summary in section 4.

Sources

SourceVersion usedNotes
Case descriptionNotion page “Case Product / Tech”Defines scope and deliverables.

Method

Every route was analysed with the same seven questions, so the results can be compared and the approach reused for other connectors:

  1. Business meaning: which object or action does the Chift route represent?
  2. Chift contract: what is required on input and promised on output?
  3. Horus capability: does Horus hold this object, and can it be read or written through the API?
  4. Endpoints: which Horus calls, in which order, and how many per Chift request?
  5. Attributes: direct, transformed, looked up, composite, constant or unavailable?
  6. Behaviour: filters, pagination, numbering, status and incremental sync: native or emulated?
  7. Risk: limitations, edge cases and error handling.

Every cited endpoint, field and value was checked against the documentation, and each route was checked separately for missing attributes, endpoints and edge cases. A final pass aligned the routes with each other, so that what one route writes, another reads back the same way; the resulting cross-route working assumptions are listed in appendix B.

How to read this report

Verdicts

  • Supported One native Horus endpoint, plus cached reference data, serves the route with deterministic transformations. Losses are limited to minor optional fields.
  • Supported with workaround Feasible, but needs extra calls per request (read-backs, joins, loops), emulated filters, pagination or numbering, or loses a capability clients typically rely on.
  • Not supported No Horus capability for the route.
  • To confirm The documentation is insufficient to decide.

Basis of each statement

  • Documented Stated in the Chift or Horus documentation.
  • Derived Concluded from documented facts.
  • Assumption Plausible but not documented; to validate.
  • To confirm Missing or ambiguous in the documentation.

In the detailed texts these labels appear in brackets, for example (documented) or (assumption). References such as (D7) point to the cross-route working assumptions in appendix B.

Endpoint roles (route cards)

Primary: the call that performs the route. Follow-up: the read-back or next call after the primary one. Lookup: reference data used to resolve ids, usually cached. Enrichment: extra data for optional response fields. Alternative: can replace another listed call. Optional: not needed in the recommended design.

Mapping types (attribute tables)

Direct same meaning and format Transform format, code or sign conversion Lookup resolved through another call Composite built from several fields or calls Constant set by the connector Not available no Horus equivalent Chift-side handled inside Chift

3How Horus works

Horus Office is used by accounting firms (fiduciaries) and SMBs. The Horus Software API reads and writes the data of one Horus Office database. Three structural facts shape the connector: each license has its own database and API URL, almost every call is scoped to one folder, and accounting entries are created through business-specific endpoints (invoices, bank, financial, miscellaneous operations) that all return a book entry id.

Concept map: Chift ↔ Horus

The same word can mean different things in the two systems. This alignment is the basis of every mapping below.

Chift conceptHorus conceptWhat matters for the connectorBasis
Consumer connectionLicense + OAuth connectionEach license (an accounting firm or an SMB) has its own database and its own API URL, returned at the end of the OAuth flow.Documented
Folder (legal entity)FolderThe accounting record of one legal entity (for an accounting firm, one of its clients). Almost every endpoint requires a FolderId.Documented
Client / supplierCompany, flagged IsCustomer / IsSupplierIn Horus, “company” means a third party. One record can be both customer and supplier.Documented
JournalDaybookThe daybook type decides which posting endpoint is used (sales, purchases, bank, financial, miscellaneous).Documented
Ledger accountAccount, identified by its search keyPostings reference accounts by search key (usually the account number), not by id.Documented
Analytic plan / analytic accountCost schema / cost sectionRequires the folder’s analytics module. Up to 8 cost schemas per folder.Documented
Analytic distributionCost lines (amounts) or a cost distribution keyHorus allocates amounts to combinations of cost sections; Chift sends percentages per plan.Documented
Sale/purchase entryBook entry created from a sales or purchase invoiceType 0 = invoice, 1 = credit note. Creation returns only the book entry id.Documented
Entry linesAccount history linesOne line per account movement, with debit/credit, VAT detail, matching number and cost detail.Documented
VAT codeNo VAT-code entity: rate + origin + type (+ kind and condition for purchases)Only some combinations are valid; Horus lists them in reference spreadsheets.Documented
Miscellaneous operationMiscellaneous operation (OD) book entryDebit and credit lines that must balance.Documented
BookyearFiscal yearCarries closure dates per daybook type; Horus recommends checking them before posting.Documented
Posted / draftConfirmed true / falseAPI entries are unconfirmed by default so the accountant can review them before confirming.Documented
Outstanding itemUnmatched account history line, or open sales invoiceLines not yet matched make up the outstanding items.Documented
AttachmentDocument (main document and annexes)Sent as multipart/form-data with the entry, or attached afterwards.Documented

Horus conventions that shape every route

TopicConventionBasis
AuthenticationOAuth 2 authorisation code flow through my-horus.com. The user picks the license; the code is exchanged with grant_type=auth_code (not the usual authorization_code). Access tokens last one hour; refresh tokens are single-use, so refreshes must be serialised per connection.Documented
Base URLOne API URL per license, received during authorisation. It must be stored with the connection and never hard-coded.Documented
Request styleJSON-RPC style methods ({api_url}/{category}.{action}). Simple reads use GET with a query string; lists with filters, creations and updates use POST with a JSON body.Documented
Response envelopePayload under Data. Related entities are references {Type, Id}. Creations and updates return only {Id}.Documented
SideloadingAn Include parameter returns related entities under Included, on four endpoints only: book-entries.list, account-histories.list, companies.list and invoices.sales.outstanding.list.Documented
PaginationNo list endpoint has paging parameters and the guides describe none. Lists return the full result, so the connector must emulate Chift’s page, size and total.Derived
FiltersString filters use “contains”; all filters must match. Many lists offer ModifiedAfter/ModifiedBefore for incremental synchronisation. Ledger periods are expressed in months.Documented
UpdatesMaster data uses partial updates (null keeps a value, an empty string erases it). Invoice and operation updates (.update) are full replacements that create a new book entry id and drop the original attachment. Exception: book-entries.miscellaneous-operations.lines.update changes some lines in place (no amount changes).Documented
ErrorsErrors other than authorisation errors (401) return {ErrorType, Details, Message}. Status codes: 400, 401, 403, 404, 500. Horus advises: refresh once on 401, do not retry 400, back off on 500. The list of ErrorType values is not documented.Documented
VersioningNew endpoints, optional parameters and response properties can appear without notice; breaking changes are announced by email. The connector must ignore unknown properties.Documented
TestingRequests run against the license’s real database. Write tests need a test license or test folder; no sandbox is documented.Documented

Open a route for its endpoints, call sequence, considerations and key attribute mapping.
Client creationSupported with workaroundPOST /consumers/{consumer_id}/accounting/clientsNative creation with companies.new. The connector generates the Horus search key, derives required flags, and reads the company back to build the Chift response.

A Chift client becomes a Horus company (third party) flagged as customer, created with companies.new. The connector generates the required search key and fills the Horus-required flags, language and country from Chift data or folder defaults. Creation returns only an id, so the company is read back (two to three calls) to build the Chift response. force_merge flags an existing supplier as customer instead of creating a duplicate. Main risks: undocumented search-key rules, duplicate VAT numbers refused by default, no idempotency key, and one address per company.

Detailed analysis

A Chift client is a Horus 'company', the single third-party record of a Horus folder, flagged IsCustomer/IsSupplier (Guide: Data model: "Companies are the legal entities a folder deals with: customers, suppliers and beneficiaries"). A Horus 'folder' corresponds to Chift's folder_id ("the accounting record of one company"), resolved with the shared folder rule (D7: required when the connection has several selected folders; otherwise the single selected folder, or the single folder visible through folders.list Mode 0). The primary call is POST /companies.new with FolderId in the JSON body. Of its 8 required fields, SearchKey, IsCustomer and IsSupplier have no Chift source. IsVatLiable is derived from vat. LanguageCode and CountryCode come from optional Chift data, with folder defaults as fallback (D9 write-side language fallback). Create calls "return only its identifier" (Guide: Responses & errors), so ClientItemOut needs a read-back: companies.info + ibans.list + account SearchKey resolution (2-3 calls), or possibly one companies.list call with Filter.Id + Include (sideloading is supported on companies.list, but which include paths work is undocumented, A). The response is built with the shared Horus company → Chift partner mapper (D9) that Invoice retrieval and Invoice creation also use, and every Id-only Horus call is guarded by a Data.Folder.Id check (D13). With the optional pre-checks (duplicate VAT/ExternalReference, free SearchKey, account validation), one create costs about 3-7 calls. force_merge makes sense because Horus uses one object for both roles: companies.update {IsCustomer:true} (partial update, never "") plus ibans.new. Main risks: SearchKey generation (unique within the folder, but length/charset undocumented); duplicate VAT refused by default; a non-idempotent create, so no blind retry on 500/timeout despite the guide's generic backoff advice; a single flat address; several Chift fields with no writable Horus field (and is_company always read back as true); undocumented ErrorType values, pagination and rate limits.

Horus endpoints

  • PrimaryPOST /companies.new Creates the customer as a Horus company (third party) in the folder. Returns only its GUID. (1 per Chift call (normal path; skipped when force_merge is used))
  • Follow-upGET /companies.info Documented read-back after create or merge: scalar fields, LastModifiedDate, SearchKey, ContactPhone, MainIban, Ibans refs, CustomerConcentrationAccount ref, Folder. Cross-folder guard (D13): companies.info takes only an Id, so on every call the connector checks Data.Folder.Id = FolderId and returns 400 otherwise. In the force_merge path it also validates the existing supplier (existence, folder, IsSupplier) before the Id-only companies.update. (1 per Chift call (+1 in the force_merge path for validation); replaceable by the companies.list read-back alternative (assumption))
  • AlternativePOST /companies.list Alternative single-call read-back: {FolderId, Filter:{Id:<new Id>}, Include:"MainIban,Ibans,CustomerConcentrationAccount"} would return the scalar fields plus the IBAN and account entities under Included (indexed by Type 'Ibans' / 'Accounts'). The same Filter.Id call, without Include, is a folder-scoped existence check for force_merge. Because FolderId is required in its body ("Get a list of companies for a folder."), this call is folder-scoped by construction and satisfies the D13 guard without a separate check; Data[].Folder can still be asserted. (0-1 per Chift call (replaces companies.info + ibans.list + accounts.info if the include paths work, A))
  • EnrichmentPOST /ibans.list Returns the company's IBANs (Id, Iban, Bic) to fill bank_accounts[] and the deprecated iban field. In the force_merge path, avoids re-adding an IBAN that already exists. It takes only CompanyId and returns no Folder, so it is called only for a company whose Data.Folder.Id was verified on companies.info (D13 guard inherited from the company). (0-1 per Chift call (skip when companies.info Data.Ibans is empty))
  • EnrichmentGET /accounts.info Resolves CustomerConcentrationAccount {Type:'Accounts', Id} into the account SearchKey (e.g. 400000) for response.account_number, when the folder's accounts.list is not cached. Id-only call: check Data.Folder.Id = FolderId, otherwise 400 (D13). The folder-scoped accounts.list cache is preferred. (0-1 per Chift call; usually replaced by the per-folder accounts.list cache)
  • LookupPOST /accounts.list Per-folder reference cache of the chart of accounts. Validates the Chift account_number (exact SearchKey match, not Inactive) before it is sent as CustomerConcentrationAccount, and resolves account Ids to SearchKeys on read-back. (cached reference data per folder (0-1 live call per Chift call when the cache is cold))
  • LookupPOST /companies.list Pre-checks: duplicate VAT (equals, IsVatLiable:true), duplicate ExternalReference (contains → exact post-filter), free SearchKey (contains → exact post-filter), optional duplicate IBAN (equals). Also the check-before-retry after a timeout or 500 on companies.new. (1-3 per Chift call (one per check performed))
  • AlternativePOST /companies.update force_merge path: sets IsCustomer=true (plus customer-only fields) on the existing supplier instead of creating a duplicate. Partial update: null/omitted keeps the value, "" erases it. It carries only CompanyId (no FolderId), so it is sent only after companies.info confirmed Data.Folder.Id = FolderId (D13). (0-1 per Chift call (only when force_merge is set))
  • AlternativePOST /ibans.new force_merge path only: adds the client's IBAN(s) to the existing company (companies.update has no Ibans field) (0-2 per Chift call (one per new IBAN, merge path only))
  • LookupPOST /folders.list D7 fallback only: when folder_id is absent and no folder selection is stored for the connection, use the single folder returned by Mode 0; several folders → 400 'folder_id required'. Can also confirm that a folder_id is visible to the connected Horus user. (cached reference data (per connection); needed only when no activation selection is stored (D7))
  • LookupGET /folders.info Folder defaults for required company fields: Language (LanguageCode fallback) and CountryCode (fallback when addresses[] is empty) (cached reference data (per folder))
  • LookupPOST /countries.list Checks that the CountryCode exists in Horus (reference set of country codes) (cached reference data (per connection))
  • OptionalGET /ibans.info Alternative to ibans.list to resolve MainIban.Id into Iban/Bic for the deprecated response.iban. It returns no Folder, so it is used only for an Id taken from the MainIban/Ibans refs of a company already verified on companies.info (D13 guard inherited). (0-1 per Chift call)
  • OptionalPOST /accounts.new NOT used by default. Only if Chift or the consumer wants a dedicated receivable account per client: create the account, then pass its SearchKey as CustomerConcentrationAccount. Whether Horus accepts a non-collective account there is unknown. (0 by default (0-1 per Chift call under an explicit setting))
  • OptionalPOST {api_url}/oauth2/access_token Token refresh (grant_type=refresh_token) for the consumer's connection, done proactively before expiry or once after a 401. Documented in the guides, not in the API reference endpoint list. (0-1 per Chift call (only near expiry or after a 401; serialised per connection))

Call sequence

  1. Connection and conventions. consumer_id → stored connection record for one Horus license (api_url, access_token, refresh_token, expiry; Guide: Authentication overview, 'one record per connected license'). If expiry is a few minutes away, refresh through POST {api_url}/oauth2/access_token (grant_type=refresh_token) under a per-connection lock, and persist the new pair before using it, because refresh tokens are single-use. Every call: HTTPS to api_url, Authorization: Bearer, Content-Type: application/json, optional gzip. .info = GET with ?Id=; .list/.new/.update = POST with a JSON body. Deserialise responses tolerantly (ignore unknown properties, never rely on property order).
  2. Resolve the folder (D7). folder_id given → FolderId; reject with 400 if it is not among the folders selected at Chift activation (Chift FolderItem.selected: "Requests targeting a folder that was not selected will be rejected"). folder_id absent → if exactly one folder was selected at activation, use it; if no selection is stored, POST /folders.list {Mode:0} (cached) and use the folder if exactly one is returned; otherwise (several selected or visible folders) reject with 400 'folder_id required'. GET /folders.info?Id=FolderId (cached) gives the Language and CountryCode defaults. Horus 403 → Chift 400 'folder not accessible with this connection'.
  3. Local validation and transformation (Chift 422 before any Horus call). Pick one address (main > invoice > delivery). Build Address = street + ' ' + number (+ '/' + box). Map country → uppercase alpha-2 CountryCode, checked against the cached countries.list. Map language → LanguageCode (FR/NL/DE/EN/ES/PT, otherwise the folder Language, possibly 'XX': the D9 write-side fallback shared by every write route). Normalise vat (uppercase, strip spaces/dots/dashes); if malformed → 422 ERROR_INVALID_VAT_FORMAT. IsVatLiable = vat present. Strip and uppercase the IBAN(s).
  4. If account_number is given: look it up in the folder's cached accounts.list, or call POST /accounts.list {FolderId, Filter:{SearchKeys:[account_number]}} ('starts with' → keep the exact SearchKey match only). No match, or Inactive=true → 400. Never create accounts implicitly.
  5. If force_merge is set: GET /companies.info?Id={force_merge} and check Data.Folder.Id = FolderId (D13), or POST /companies.list {FolderId, Filter:{Id: force_merge}} (folder-scoped by its FolderId). Reject with 400 if it returns 404/empty, if Folder.Id ≠ FolderId, or if IsSupplier = false. If IsCustomer is already true, skip the update. Otherwise POST /companies.update (Id-only call, sent only after the folder check) {CompanyId: force_merge, IsCustomer: true, CustomerConcentrationAccount (if given), ExternalReference (if given and policy allows)}, with every other field null/omitted and never "" ("" erases data). Then POST /ibans.list {CompanyId}, and POST /ibans.new {FolderId, CompanyId, Iban} for each Chift IBAN not already present (omit IsDefault so the supplier's main IBAN is kept). Set Id = force_merge and go to step 7.
  6. Duplicate pre-check (normal path). If vat: POST /companies.list {FolderId, Filter:{IsVatLiable:true, VatNumber}} (equals). If external_reference: POST /companies.list {FolderId, Filter:{ExternalReference}}, keep exact matches only. Match with IsCustomer=true → 400 ERROR_PARTNER_ALREADY_EXISTS (existing_code = Horus SearchKey/Id). Supplier-only match → 400 ERROR_PARTNER_ALREADY_EXISTS whose message invites a retry with force_merge={Id}. Match that is beneficiary-only or has neither flag → 400 with the Id; never merge silently.
  7. SearchKey: derive a candidate from name (uppercase, ASCII, A-Z0-9 only, truncated; assumed rule). POST /companies.list {FolderId, Filter:{SearchKey:candidate}} and post-filter for an exact match (search keys are 'unique within the folder'). On collision, append a numeric suffix and check again.
  8. POST /companies.new {FolderId, SearchKey, Name, IsCustomer:true, IsSupplier:false, IsVatLiable, VatNumber?, AllowDuplicateVatNumber:false, LanguageCode, CountryCode, Address?, ZipCode?, Locality?, PhoneNumber?, Website?, ContactEmail?, InvoiceEmail?, ExternalReference?, CustomerConcentrationAccount?, CustomerActivitySuspended?, Ibans?:[{Iban, IsDefault:true, IsDomiciliation:false}]} → Id. Error handling: 400 → do not retry as-is. Log ErrorType/Details. Regenerate the SearchKey once only if ErrorType/Details points to a SearchKey conflict (ErrorType values undocumented). A duplicate VAT → 400 ERROR_PARTNER_ALREADY_EXISTS. Anything else → Chift 400 with Details. 401 → refresh once and replay (the token was rejected, so the create was not processed, D); if the refresh fails, mark the connection disconnected. 403 → Chift 400 folder not accessible. 500, timeout or network error → do NOT apply the generic exponential-backoff retry blindly. First search companies.list by ExternalReference (exact), then VatNumber (equals), then the generated SearchKey (exact), and re-POST only if nothing is found.
  9. Read-back. Documented path: GET /companies.info?Id={Id}, then check Data.Folder.Id = FolderId (D13). A mismatch should never happen because FolderId was sent on create; if it does, return 400 with the created Id in the error detail, log it, and never re-POST. The response gives all scalar fields, LastModifiedDate, SearchKey, MainIban, Ibans refs and the CustomerConcentrationAccount ref. Alternative to test (assumption): POST /companies.list {FolderId, Filter:{Id}, Include:"MainIban,Ibans,CustomerConcentrationAccount"} (folder-scoped by construction), resolving the refs from Included by Type then Id. That would replace steps 7-9 with one call.
  10. POST /ibans.list {CompanyId: Id} → bank_accounts[] (is_primary = Data[].Id == MainIban.Id). ibans.list has no FolderId and returns no Folder, so its folder guard is the step-7 check on the company (D13). Skip when companies.info Data.Ibans is empty.
  11. If Data.CustomerConcentrationAccount is not null: resolve its Id through the cached, folder-scoped accounts.list (Data[].Id → SearchKey), or call GET /accounts.info?Id=… and check Data.Folder.Id = FolderId (D13, else 400) → account_number. Expect a value even when account_number was omitted, because Horus applies a default (derived).
  12. Build ClientItemOut from steps 7-9 with the shared Horus company → Chift partner mapper (D9): active = NOT CustomerActivitySuspended (assumption), true when absent; one 'main' address from Address (+Address2), ZipCode, Locality, CountryCode, with address-level phone/email = null; phone = ContactPhone; email = ContactEmail; is_company = true; language = lowercase Horus Language, 'XX' → null; third_party_account = company SearchKey; account_number = CustomerConcentrationAccount resolved to its SearchKey. The response is mapped from the read-back, not from the request. If companies.new succeeded but the read-back keeps failing, return at least id plus the echoed request values and log the failure. Never re-POST.

Considerations and limitations

  • A Horus company has a single flat address (Address/Address2/ZipCode/Locality/CountryCode). The flat fields are documented; the absence of any address list or address type is deduced from the companies.new/update schemas in the current API reference (optional fields may be added without notice, Guide: Changes & versioning). Impact: Only one address is kept (main > invoice > delivery). The other addresses are lost, and Get clients will return one 'main' address. Derived
  • No Horus write field for mobile, internal_notes, function, first_name, last_name, company_id or currency (CurrencyCode is read-only on companies.info). Deduced from the absence of such fields in POST /companies.new / companies.update in the current API reference. Impact: These values are dropped and the response returns null for them; callers should not expect a round-trip. Derived
  • company_number has no write field. EnterpriseNumber is returned only when IsVatLiable=false. Impact: The enterprise number of a VAT-liable client is not stored separately. For non-VAT-liable clients it can be stored only if Horus confirms that VatNumber + IsVatLiable=false holds the enterprise number. Assumption
  • No company/individual flag and no active/inactive flag on companies; the only candidate is the undocumented CustomerActivitySuspended. Impact: is_company is not persisted: Create client and every later read return is_company=true (D9), so a client created as an individual (is_company=false) comes back as a company. active=false can be emulated only if Horus confirms the meaning of CustomerActivitySuspended (read as active = NOT CustomerActivitySuspended, D9). Assumption
  • Horus bank accounts are IBANs only (Iban + Bic); there is no local account number, routing code or currency per bank account (deduced from ibans.* and companies.new Ibans schemas). Impact: Non-IBAN bank_account values (US/CA/AU style) are lost. bank_accounts[].account_number/routing_code/currency are always null. Derived
  • companies.new returns only {Id} (reference RESPONSE 200, and Guide: Responses & errors: "Requests that create or update an entity return only its identifier"). Impact: 1-3 extra read calls are needed to return ClientItemOut: companies.info + ibans.list + an account lookup (cacheable), or possibly one companies.list call with Include (assumption). Latency, and exposure to undocumented rate limits, increase. Documented
  • SearchKey is required by Horus, has no Chift field, and must be unique within the folder (Guide: Data model). Impact: The connector must generate it and check for collisions per folder. Users may dislike generated codes, and the undocumented length/charset rules may cause 400s. Documented
  • Duplicate VAT numbers are refused by default (AllowDuplicateVatNumber "False by default"; Guide: Managing customers & suppliers: "By default, a VAT number already used by another company is refused."). Impact: Creating a client whose VAT already exists (e.g. as a supplier) fails unless force_merge is used. A pre-check is needed to return ERROR_PARTNER_ALREADY_EXISTS with the existing Id. The scope of the check (folder or license) and its ErrorType are to confirm. Documented
  • Most string filters use 'contains' (Guide: Making requests; companies.list Filter); accounts.list SearchKeys uses 'starts with'. Exact matching is documented only for VatNumber, Iban and CountryCodes (equals). Filter.Id is exact by construction on a full 36-character GUID (derived). Impact: Duplicate, ExternalReference and SearchKey checks need client-side exact post-filtering. Documented
  • Pagination, page size and rate limits are not documented in the API reference or the guides. Making requests covers naming, headers, filters, dates, partial updates and gzip only, and no list endpoint has page parameters. Impact: The connector assumes lists return every match (assumption); short SearchKey candidates may return large sets. Each Chift create costs about 3-7 calls with no known quota. Ask Horus. To confirm
  • companies.new is not idempotent: the request schema has no idempotency key, and neither the reference nor the guides mention idempotency. The guide's generic '500 : retry later with exponential backoff' must therefore be overridden for this call. Impact: A blind retry after a 500, timeout or network error can create duplicate companies. The connector must search (ExternalReference → VatNumber → SearchKey) before re-posting. Derived
  • The ErrorType catalogue is not documented: only the envelope {ErrorType, Details, Message} and the example 'not_found' are. Impact: Horus 400s cannot be mapped reliably to specific Chift errors (duplicate VAT vs duplicate SearchKey vs invalid IBAN). The connector relies on its own pre-checks plus a generic 400 that carries Details. To confirm
  • Writes run against the license's real database (Guide: Testing the API), and no separate sandbox environment is documented. The 72-endpoint reference has no companies.delete (ibans.delete / ibans.clear exist). Impact: QA of Create client needs a dedicated test license or test folder from Horus. Test companies cannot be removed through the API (derived); only their IBANs can. Documented
  • Several Horus customer fields have no Chift source in ClientItemIn: CustomerPaymentDelay, UsualCustomerPaymentMode, domiciliation fields, ReminderEmail, Family, Name2, ContactPerson. Impact: They stay at Horus defaults. Payment terms must be managed elsewhere. Documented
  • Horus Id-only calls (companies.info, accounts.info, ibans.list, ibans.info, companies.update) take a GUID without any FolderId, while one license connection can see several folders. companies.info and accounts.info return Data.Folder; ibans.list/ibans.info return no Folder, and companies.update returns only Id. Impact: Without a connector-side guard, a GUID from another folder of the same license could be read or updated. The connector checks Data.Folder.Id = FolderId on companies.info/accounts.info (400 otherwise) and calls ibans.* and companies.update only for a company that passed this check (D13). Derived
  • ClientItemOut is built with the shared Horus company → Chift partner mapper (D9), which exposes only ContactPhone/ContactEmail at root, one 'main' address, is_company = true and language with 'XX' → null. Impact: InvoiceEmail and ReminderEmail are not returned (a company with only an InvoiceEmail returns email = null), address-level phone/email are always null, and a folder-fallback language 'XX' written on create reads back as null. Derived
  • Full attribute-level mapping in section 5 (Client creation). Derived
Detailed open questions (19)
  • Horus Company SearchKey: what are the max length, the allowed characters and the case sensitivity? Does Horus generate a key when it is empty, and which ErrorType/Details does companies.new return for a duplicate SearchKey? SearchKey is required and has no Chift source. Uniqueness within the folder is documented; the rest is not, and a wrong generation rule causes 400s.
  • Horus Which ErrorType values does companies.new return for validation failures: duplicate VAT, duplicate SearchKey, invalid IBAN, unknown or inactive concentration account, invalid LanguageCode/CountryCode? Turns generic Horus 400s into actionable Chift errors (e.g. ERROR_PARTNER_ALREADY_EXISTS), decides when a SearchKey retry is allowed, and could make some pre-checks optional.
  • Horus Is the duplicate-VAT check per folder or per license? Drives the pre-check design. Companies belong to a folder, so a per-folder check is likely (derived), but it is not stated.
  • Horus Which LanguageCode values does companies.new accept, and what does 'XX' mean, given that the company enum lists both 'EN' and 'XX' and countries.list labels CountryNameXx as English? LanguageCode is required. Only the read/filter enum is documented, and the folder enum (FR/NL/DE/XX) has no EN. D9 sends the folder value as-is on write and returns 'XX' as null on read.
  • Horus Is CountryCode always ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 (e.g. GR vs EL, GB vs UK)? Needed for a deterministic ISO → Horus transformation.
  • Horus How is a company's enterprise (local business) number written: VatNumber with IsVatLiable=false? Can it be stored for VAT-liable companies? Guide: Data model says companies are identified by 'a VAT number and/or a local business number', and companies.info returns EnterpriseNumber, but companies.new/update have no such field.
  • Horus Can a company's CurrencyCode be set via the API? It is returned by companies.info but absent from companies.new/update.
  • Horus What does CustomerActivitySuspended mean, and is there an 'inactive' flag for companies? Needed to map Chift active: the shared mapper reads active = NOT CustomerActivitySuspended (D9) and stays an assumption until Horus confirms.
  • Horus Is companies.new atomic with its Ibans array (an invalid IBAN means no company is created)? Are duplicate IBANs across companies refused? Decides whether to send IBANs inline or after creation via ibans.new, and whether an IBAN pre-check is needed.
  • Horus Which CustomerConcentrationAccount is applied when it is omitted (folder setting? 400000?), must it be a collective (IntCategory COLLECCUS) account, and can a dedicated per-customer account created with accounts.new be used? The guide implies a default exists ('optional … set explicitly'). The answer defines the semantics of account_number, the validation rule, and whether accounts.new is ever needed.
  • Horus What do Name2, Address2, Family and ContactPerson mean, and what are the field length limits? Needed to place box, address name, person names and long names without truncation.
  • Horus Which relationship paths can be included on companies.list (MainIban, Ibans, CustomerConcentrationAccount), and which fields do the Included Ibans/Accounts entities carry (Iban/Bic? SearchKey?)? If supported, the read-back shrinks from 3 calls to 1 (one create = 2 calls in the best case).
  • Horus Do pagination, a maximum page size or rate limits apply to companies.list / accounts.list and per license? Neither the reference nor the guides mention them. They affect pre-check completeness and the call budget per create.
  • Horus Can Horus provide a test license or test folder for connector QA, and can test companies be deleted (no companies.delete in the API)? Guide: Testing the API says requests run against the license's real database.
  • Chift force_merge policy: on merge, which supplier fields may be overwritten with client data (name, address, contacts, VAT), or only IsCustomer and customer-specific fields? May force_merge target a beneficiary-only or flagless company? companies.update overwrites what is sent. The business rule must match other Chift connectors.
  • Chift On a duplicate VAT/external_reference, should the connector return 400 ERROR_PARTNER_ALREADY_EXISTS or the existing client? The unified API should behave consistently across connectors.
  • Chift Can the connector return warnings for dropped or non-round-tripped data (extra addresses, mobile, notes, currency, is_company=false, which D9 always returns as true, and a language that falls back to the folder language)? Several fields are lost. D9 fixes is_company = true and 'XX' → null across routes, but callers still need a transparent contract.
  • Chift Should SearchKey generation be configurable per consumer (e.g. from external_reference or a prefix scheme)? Accountants often have coding conventions for third-party codes. The same rules are reused when Invoice creation creates a partner from partner_info.account_number (D10), so the choice affects both routes.
  • Horus For a FolderId the user cannot access, or a company/account Id that belongs to another folder of the license, does Horus return 403, 404 or 400? Are Id-only calls (companies.info, ibans.list, companies.update) restricted to a folder server-side? D7 maps Horus 403 to Chift 400 'folder not accessible with this connection', and D13 adds a connector-side Folder.Id check because the .info/.update calls take only a GUID. The answer confirms the error mapping and how much the guard protects.
Invoice retrieval (multiple analytic plans)Supported with workaroundGET /consumers/{consumer_id}/accounting/invoices/multi-analytic-plans/type/{invoice_type}Horus has no invoice list. Invoices are rebuilt from book entries, account history lines and open-item data, with pagination and day-level filters emulated.

Horus has no endpoint that lists invoices: posted invoices become book entries. The connector reads the entry headers per daybook (sales or purchases, invoices and credit notes), then all lines with VAT, analytics and matching in one ledger call, plus open sales items for payments and document type, and assembles Chift invoices. Pagination, day-level date filters and payment status are emulated. Main risks: invoice/credit-note classification for closed or purchase entries, ids that change when the accountant edits an entry, and no line quantities.

Detailed analysis

Horus has no endpoint that reads sales or purchase invoices. The 72-endpoint reference only offers invoices.*.new/update and invoices.sales.outstanding.list/info, which return open sales items only, and pushed invoices 'are converted into book entries in the corresponding daybook' (Guide: Posting sales & purchase invoices), so each Chift invoice must be rebuilt from the ledger. The connector caches every daybook (daybooks.list) and, per D1, ALWAYS reads both sibling daybook types (customer_* → 200 Sale + 250 SaleCreditNote; supplier_* → 100 Purchase + 150 PurchaseCreditNote), one book-entries.list call per daybook because DaybookId is singular and required, with the partner sideloaded through Include 'CompanyDetail.Company'. One account-histories.list call returns all lines, VAT, analytics, the partner ledger account, due dates and matching state, grouped by BookEntry.Id. Every entry is then classified by the partner-line sign (customer: net debit = invoice, net credit = credit note; supplier: net credit = invoice, net debit = credit note); for sales, TransactionType from invoices.sales.outstanding.list (loaded once per sales request) overrides the sign on open items. This classification is mandatory and treated as an assumption until Horus confirms it or exposes the document Type on read. Payments come from a second account-histories.list call on the partner ledger accounts across all daybooks, joined on (company, account type, MatchNo); payments[].journal_type follows the shared daybook table (D6). Shared conventions: lines[].tax_code is the canonical synthetic VAT id (D2), returned only when exactly one catalogue entry matches, otherwise null; reference and customer_memo come from splitting the header comment on the first ' | ' (D3, read location A); amounts are treated as folder-currency EUR and a foreign-currency invoice is returned in its own currency only when the open-sales list gives TotalCurrencyAmount, otherwise in EUR with currency 'EUR' (D4); the partner uses the shared company mapper (D9); analytics come from the shared per-folder cache with Free schemas omitted (D8); folder_id follows D7 and every Id-only call checks Data.Folder.Id (D13). Pagination, day-level dates, payment_status and approval_status are emulated because Horus has no pagination and periods are whole months. The main risks: every update creates a new book entry Id and deletions leave no signal, the invoice/credit-note split rests on an undocumented sign rule, lines have no quantity or unit price, per-line tax_amount, tax_code identity (null when TaxKind is ambiguous) and payment allocation are approximations, foreign-currency amounts depend on an unconfirmed assumption, and 'paid' is inferred from full matching. GET /book-entries.info reads one entry by Id without a daybook or period, but returns only the header (lines as references, no sideloading), so it serves single-invoice reads and the attachments Document lookup, not the list path.

Horus endpoints

  • LookupPOST /daybooks.list Called WITHOUT a Types filter and cached in full per folder. The connector selects the daybooks of BOTH sibling types client-side (D1): customer_invoice / customer_refund → Types 200 + 250; supplier_invoice / supplier_refund → Types 100 + 150. It validates journal_ids against that pair and builds a map keyed by Id and by SearchKey. Payment lines only carry DaybookSearchKey, so this map resolves payments[].journal_id, journal_name (Heading) and journal_type (Type, through the shared D6 table) for any daybook type. (cached reference data (1 per folder, per TTL))
  • PrimaryPOST /book-entries.list Invoice header (the anchor object): Id, DocumentNo, Date, Confirmed, Verified, EntryType, LastModifiedDate, CompanyDetail (Company, DueDate, PaymentDate, TotalAmount, TurnoverAmount, TaxAmount, RemarkPayment, RemarkCompany, DiscountDetail), Daybook, Document and AccountHistories refs. Called for every daybook of both sibling types (D1), because a credit note may sit in a 200/100 daybook and an invoice in a 250/150 one. RemarkCompany is the second source of the header comment (D3). CompanyDetail amounts are taken as folder-currency EUR (D4, A). Supports Include sideloading of CompanyDetail.Company. The per-daybook calls can run in parallel. (1 per daybook of the two sibling types (200+250 or 100+150, after journal_ids) per Chift request (DaybookId is required and singular; D1))
  • Follow-upGET /book-entries.info One book entry by Id, with no DaybookId or month window. The response has the same fields as a book-entries.list item: Id, DocumentNo, Confirmed, Verified, EntryType, OperationType, Date, LastModifiedDate, CompanyDetail (Company ref, totals, DueDate, remarks, DiscountDetail), Folder, Daybook, Document and AccountHistories refs. Used (a) to resolve BookEntry.Id → Document.Id for the follow-up attachments route, and (b) as the header call of a single-invoice read or refresh, where Date sets the month window of the account-histories.list Filter.BookEntryId call and Daybook.Id gives the invoice type through the cached daybook map. Not used in the list path: calling it per entry after book-entries.list adds no field (N+1 with no gain). It returns no lines, VAT, currency or matching (AccountHistories are {Type, Id} refs only), and sideloading is not supported on it, so the partner needs companies.info or the cached companies.list. Cross-folder guard (D13): the connector compares Data.Folder.Id with the request's folder_id and returns 400 when they differ, because the call takes only an Id. (0 in the list path; 1 per invoice on demand (single-invoice read or attachments route))
  • PrimaryPOST /account-histories.list Call 1 (all daybooks of both sibling types via DaybookIds, D1): all lines of the candidate entries. Partner lines (AccountType 100/200) give the net debit/credit sign that classifies each entry as invoice or credit note (D1), CurrencyCode (foreign-currency detection, D4), the partner ledger account, DueDate, MatchNo/MatchStatus and the partner-line Comment (first source of the header comment, D3). Other non-VAT lines give the Chift lines, VAT lines (400) the tax amounts, VatDetail the rate and the components of the synthetic tax_code (D2), CostDetail the analytics (D8). Call 2 (payments): lines on the partner ledger accounts (Filter.AccountSearchKeys) across ALL daybooks over a wider window, joined on (Company.Id, AccountType, MatchNo). (1 per Chift request for invoice lines; +1 when include_payments=true or payment_status!=all)
  • AlternativePOST /account-histories.list Variant with Filter.BookEntryId: all lines of ONE book entry (with VatDetail, CostDetail, MatchDetail and the Document ref) in one call. It is the line call of a single-invoice read or refresh, made after GET /book-entries.info. The month window is still mandatory, so it is set from the book-entries.info Date (that month, optionally widened by 1 month on each side because the period basis is unconfirmed, A). The attachments route no longer needs this call, because book-entries.info returns the Document ref without a period. The partner-line sign of these lines classifies the single entry as invoice or credit note (D1). (1 per invoice, only for a single-invoice read or refresh (never in the list path))
  • EnrichmentPOST /invoices.sales.outstanding.list Sales types only, loaded once per request (cached per folder briefly) and joined on BookEntry.Id. TransactionType ('0: Sale invoice' / '1: Sale credit note') overrides the partner-line sign for open sales items in the mandatory D1 classification. Remark is the third source of the header comment (D3). TotalCurrencyAmount and Currency are the only read-side invoice-currency amounts (D4). OpenAmount, PaymentStatus, PaymentRemark and LinkedElements (payments, credit notes, miscellaneous operations) serve payments and payment_status. There is no date, daybook or ModifiedAfter filter, so each call returns every open item of the folder. (1 per Chift request for sales types (customer_invoice / customer_refund), whatever include_payments says, because D1 needs TransactionType; 0 for purchase types; payload independent of the requested period)
  • AlternativeGET /invoices.sales.outstanding.info Per-invoice version of the outstanding list (linked elements of one open sales invoice). Not used in the list path because it creates an N+1 pattern. In a single sales invoice read it replaces the whole-folder list: one call by BookEntryId gives TransactionType (D1 override), Remark (D3) and TotalCurrencyAmount (D4) for that entry; a 404 or an empty answer means the item is not open, so the sign alone decides. (1 per invoice (N+1) in the list path, avoid; 1 per single sales invoice read)
  • AlternativePOST /suppliers.outstanding Purchases: open supplier lines (BookEntry refs, MatchDetail). Not needed in the recommended design, because the partner lines from account-histories.list call 1 already carry MatchDetail. It has no period or daybook filter, so it returns every open supplier item of the folder. (1 per Chift request if used (whole folder, whatever the date range))
  • AlternativePOST /customers.outstanding Sales: open customer lines (including credit notes and miscellaneous items, plus ReminderLevel). Not needed in the recommended design (partner-line MatchDetail is enough). It has no period or daybook filter. (1 per Chift request if used (whole folder))
  • EnrichmentPOST /companies.list Partner details for the shared company → partner mapper (D9): Name, VatNumber, EnterpriseNumber, ContactEmail, ContactPhone, ContactWebsite, Address/Address2/ZipCode/Locality/CountryCode, Language, ExternalReference, CustomerActivitySuspended, concentration accounts and IBAN refs. Fallback when sideloading is not used or when sideloaded Companies objects lack fields. Folder-scoped, so no extra folder check is needed. Supports sideloading itself. (0 extra calls if sideloaded via book-entries.list Include; otherwise 1 cached call per folder)
  • AlternativeGET /companies.info Single partner fetch. Fallback only in the list path. In a single-invoice read it is the partner call, because book-entries.info does not support sideloading (the cached companies.list works too). Cross-folder guard (D13): Data.Folder.Id must equal folder_id, otherwise 400. (1 per distinct partner (N+1) in the list path, avoid; 1 per single-invoice read when the partner is needed)
  • OptionalPOST /ibans.list Partner bank_accounts (Iban, Bic). Only needed if partner.bank_accounts must be filled. (1 per distinct partner (N+1))
  • AlternativePOST /cost-histories.list Alternative source of analytics (CostSection1..8 per account-history line, joined on AccountHistory.Id) if IncludeCostDetail is unavailable or insufficient. (1 per Chift request (DaybookIds filter), no ModifiedAfter filter)
  • LookupPOST /cost-schemas.list First half of the shared per-folder analytics cache (D8). analytic_plan = cost schema GUID Id. CostSectionType tells List schemas (100) from Free schemas (900); Free schemas are excluded from Chift analytics in v1, so their cost lines are omitted from analytic_distribution with a connector warning (D8). (cached reference data (1 per folder, shared with the analytics and write routes), only when lines are returned and WithAnalytics=true)
  • LookupPOST /cost-sections.list Second half of the shared per-folder analytics cache (D8): one call per List-type schema with Filter.IncludeInactive = true. analytic_account = cost section GUID Id, which CostDetail already carries; the cache resolves CostSectionSearchKey → Id when the CostSection ref is missing. Inactive sections are still returned on read, because historic invoices may use them (the 422 rejection of inactive sections applies to the write routes only). (cached reference data (1 per List-type cost schema, max 8 per folder; CostSchemaId is required))
  • LookupGET /folders.info Folder flags WithAnalytics and IsVatLiable, which decide whether IncludeCostDetail/IncludeVatDetail are useful. There is no currency field. (cached reference data (1 per folder))
  • LookupPOST /fiscal-years.list Supplies the mandatory month window when the Chift request has no date_from/date_to (for example an updated_after-only sync). Call it with Filter.IncludeFuture=true, so entries already posted in a future fiscal year are covered. (cached reference data (1 per folder), only when dates are missing)
  • Follow-upGET /documents.download File bytes of the invoice's main document. Not called by this route: it serves the follow-up Chift GET /accounting/attachments when status='yes_to_request'. The Document Id is only ever taken from a book entry whose Data.Folder.Id was checked (D13), because documents.download takes only an Id. (1 per attachment, on demand (other Chift route))
  • OptionalGET /documents.info Optional diagnostics only: document Remark, Status, and Type (210/220/300/310). It is not part of the D1 classification (partner-line sign plus TransactionType) nor of the D3 reference rule, and it is not needed for attachments_info. If called, check Data.Folder.Id = folder_id (D13). (1 per document (N+1), avoid by default)
  • OptionalPOST /documents.list Optional bulk version of documents.info: Type (invoice vs credit note) and BookEntry ref of documents created in a window. Could serve as a diagnostic cross-check of the D1 classification or for annex discovery; it is not part of the D1 rule and is not proven to return annexes. (1 per Chift request if used (window on creation date, not invoice date))
  • AlternativeGET /account-histories.info Single line detail. Use only for debugging or as a fallback, never in the list path. Resolving the AccountHistories refs of book-entries.info this way would cost one call per line, so single-invoice reads use account-histories.list with Filter.BookEntryId instead. (1 per line (N+1), avoid)
  • LookupPOST /accounts.list Resolves a company's CustomerConcentrationAccount / SupplierConcentrationAccount Id to its SearchKey for partner.account_number in the shared D9 mapper. Needed whenever include_partner_info=true. (cached reference data (1 per folder), only when include_partner_info=true)
  • LookupPOST /folders.list D7 fallback only: when the request has no folder_id and the connection stores no folder selection, call it with Mode 0 and use the folder if exactly one is returned; otherwise return 400 'folder_id required'. (0 in the normal case; 1 per connection (cached) under the D7 fallback)

Call sequence

  1. Chift resolves consumer_id to the Horus license (its own api_url plus an OAuth token valid 1 hour) and sets FolderId by the D7 rule: folder_id when given; if absent, the single folder selected at activation; if no selection is stored, the single folder returned by POST /folders.list {Mode: 0}; otherwise 400 'folder_id required'. A Horus 403 on any call → Chift 400 'folder not accessible with this connection'.
  2. [cached per folder] GET /folders.info?Id={FolderId} returns WithAnalytics and IsVatLiable, which decide whether CostDetail and VatDetail are worth requesting. When lines are returned and WithAnalytics=true, load the shared D8 analytics cache: POST /cost-schemas.list, then POST /cost-sections.list per List-type schema with Filter.IncludeInactive=true. When include_partner_info=true, load POST /accounts.list for the D9 concentration-account SearchKey.
  3. [cached per folder] POST /daybooks.list {FolderId} with NO Types filter. Cache every daybook and index it by Id and by SearchKey. Select the daybooks of BOTH sibling types client-side (D1): customer_invoice and customer_refund → Types 200 + 250; supplier_invoice and supplier_refund → Types 100 + 150. Intersect with journal_ids (split on ','), accepting any daybook of the pair. Optionally skip daybooks with IsUsed=false (assumption: semantics undocumented).
  4. [only if date_from/date_to are missing] POST /fiscal-years.list {FolderId, Filter:{IncludeFuture:true}} to derive the mandatory StartMonth/StartYear/EndMonth/EndYear window (earliest StartDate to latest EndDate, or a Chift-defined default window).
  5. Build the month window: StartMonth/Year from date_from and EndMonth/Year from date_to, optionally widened by 1 month on each side (assumption: the Horus period may refer to AccountingMonth/AccountingYear rather than Date). Convert updated_after to ISO 8601 with '+00:00' (Guide: Making requests).
  6. For each selected daybook of the two sibling types, in parallel: POST /book-entries.list {FolderId, DaybookId, StartMonth, StartYear, EndMonth, EndYear, Filter:{ModifiedAfter?: updated_after, EntryTypes?: [100]}, Include: 'CompanyDetail.Company' when include_partner_info=true}. Returns the candidate headers plus Included.Companies. Do not call GET /book-entries.info per entry afterwards: its response has the same fields as a book-entries.list item, so it would add N calls and no data.
  7. POST /account-histories.list {FolderId, same window, Filter:{DaybookIds:[all selected daybooks of both sibling types], ExcludedEntryTypes: [0,400,600,800,810,850,900,910,1000] when step 5 uses EntryTypes [100], IncludeReport:false, IncludeCompanyDetail:true, IncludeMatchDetail:true, IncludeVatDetail:true, IncludeCostDetail: WithAnalytics}}. Do not pass ModifiedAfter here: when updated_after is set, keep only lines whose BookEntry.Id came back in step 5, so no invoice is half-built. Group the lines by BookEntry.Id. Partner lines (AccountType 100/200) give the net sign, CurrencyCode, AccountSearchKey, DueDate, MatchDetail and Comment. VAT lines (400) give the tax amounts. Every other non-report line becomes a Chift line. Check that the sum of the lines equals the header TurnoverAmount. This call is needed even when include_invoice_lines=false, because D1 classifies on the partner line.
  8. Classify (mandatory, D1, A) and filter. (a) Sales types only: POST /invoices.sales.outstanding.list {FolderId} once per request (cached per folder briefly), joined on BookEntry.Id; it also feeds D3 (Remark), D4 (TotalCurrencyAmount, Currency) and step 8. (b) Each entry: customer side, a net debit on the customer line = invoice and a net credit = credit note; supplier side, a net credit = invoice and a net debit = credit note. For open sales items, TransactionType (0 sale invoice / 1 sale credit note) overrides the sign. Entries with no partner line or a zero net fall back to the daybook Type (assumption). (c) Keep only the entries whose class matches invoice_type. Post-filter Date to [date_from, date_to] at day level. Apply approval_status (Verified) if requested.
  9. If include_payments=true or payment_status!=all: (a) sales types: reuse the step-7a invoices.sales.outstanding.list result (OpenAmount, PaymentStatus and LinkedElements per BookEntry.Id). (b) Both sides: POST /account-histories.list {FolderId, window from the invoice window start to the current month, Filter:{AccountSearchKeys:[distinct partner-line AccountSearchKeys from step 6], IncludeCompanyDetail:true, IncludeMatchDetail:true}} with NO daybook filter, so offsets in any daybook (bank, cash, financial, misc, credit notes, external) are found. Drop the invoice entries' own lines, keep lines whose (CompanyDetail.Company.Id, AccountType, MatchNo) equals an invoice partner line, and resolve each DaybookSearchKey through the step-2 map; journal_type follows the shared D6 table. (c) customers.outstanding / suppliers.outstanding are not needed, because step 6 partner lines already carry MatchDetail.
  10. Derive status (draft / posted / paid), payments[], due_dates[] (one element per partner line), attachments_info (Document ref: 'yes_to_request' or 'no') and accounting_info. Apply the shared conventions: reference / customer_memo from the header comment split on the first ' | ' (D3: partner-line Comment, then RemarkCompany, then sales Remark); lines[].tax_code from VatDetail through the D2 grammar, null unless exactly one catalogue entry matches; amounts in EUR with currency 'EUR' unless step 7a gives TotalCurrencyAmount for a foreign-currency open sales item (D4); main_currency_total = the EUR partner-line amount. Then apply the payment_status filter.
  11. Partner: map from Included.Companies (step 5) with the shared D9 mapper (active = NOT CustomerActivitySuspended, one 'main' address, is_company = true, account_number = concentration account SearchKey via the step-1 accounts.list). Fallback when it is missing or lacks properties: one cached POST /companies.list {FolderId, Filter:{IsCustomer or IsSupplier:true}}. Optional and N+1: POST /ibans.list {CompanyId} per partner for bank_accounts.
  12. Sort deterministically (Date, DaybookSearchKey, DocumentNo, Id), compute total after all post-filters, slice page/size (done by the connector), and cache the full result set briefly so later pages do not re-read Horus.
  13. Errors during the fan-out: on 401, refresh the token once (serialised per connection) and retry; on 403, return Chift 400 'folder not accessible with this connection' (D7); on 500, retry with exponential backoff (reads are idempotent). If a call still fails, fail the whole Chift request and never return a partial invoice set.
  14. Later, outside this route: Chift GET /accounting/attachments?type=invoice&document_id={id} → GET /book-entries.info?Id={id} returns Data.Document.Id in one call, with no daybook or month window. Check Data.Folder.Id = folder_id first, otherwise 400 (D13). Document null → no attachment; a 404 most likely means the Id was replaced or deleted (assumption) → GET /documents.download?Id={DocumentId} → base64.
  15. Later, outside this route: single-invoice read or refresh by Id (for example Chift GET /accounting/invoices/multi-analytic-plans/{invoice_id}, which is not part of this assignment). (a) GET /book-entries.info?Id={id} → header, Folder, Daybook.Id, Date, CompanyDetail and Document; check Data.Folder.Id = folder_id, otherwise 400 (D13). (b) POST /account-histories.list {FolderId, StartMonth/StartYear and EndMonth/EndYear = month of Date (optionally ±1 month, A), Filter:{BookEntryId: id, IncludeReport:false, IncludeCompanyDetail:true, IncludeMatchDetail:true, IncludeVatDetail:true, IncludeCostDetail: WithAnalytics}} → lines, currency, partner ledger account, DueDate, MatchDetail and the partner-line sign that classifies the entry (D1). For a 200/250 daybook, GET /invoices.sales.outstanding.info?BookEntryId={id} gives TransactionType (D1 override), Remark (D3) and TotalCurrencyAmount (D4) for an open item. (c) Partner, when needed: GET /companies.info?Id={CompanyDetail.Company.Id} with the same Folder.Id check (D13), because book-entries.info has no sideloading (or the cached companies.list). (d) Payments, when needed: the step-8b call narrowed with Filter.CompanyId and the partner line's AccountSearchKey. Total: 2 calls (3 for a sales entry), up to 5 with partner and payments, and no scan of every invoice daybook over a guessed period.

Considerations and limitations

  • Horus has no endpoint that lists or reads sales/purchase invoices. The only invoice read endpoints return open sales items (invoices.sales.outstanding.list/info). GET /book-entries.info reads one book entry by Id, but only its header: the same fields as a book-entries.list item, with lines as {Type, Id} references and no sideloading. Impact: The invoice is rebuilt from book entries and account-history lines, which means more connector logic and more calls than a native invoice read. book-entries.info only makes single-invoice reads and attachment lookups cheaper; it does not shorten the list path. Documented
  • Horus list endpoints have no pagination and no total count. Impact: Every Chift page re-reads the whole period from Horus unless the connector caches the result. Large folders over long periods give heavy payloads (gzip recommended) and possible timeouts. Derived
  • Periods are whole months (StartMonth/StartYear/EndMonth/EndYear) and are mandatory. It is unclear whether they apply to the accounting period or to Date. Impact: The connector must post-filter by day and may need to widen the window; invoices booked in a different period than their date could be missed. Assumption
  • book-entries.list accepts one DaybookId per call. Impact: Call count grows with the number of daybooks of both sibling types (D calls per request, parallelisable), because D1 requires scanning 200+250 or 100+150. Documented
  • Updating an invoice (invoices.*.update, book-entries.*.update) generates a new book entry with a new Id, and the original attachment is deleted. Impact: An updated_after sync shows the updated invoice as a new item while the old Id silently disappears; consumers must reconcile on journal_id + invoice_number. Documented
  • None of the 72 documented endpoints exposes deleted or replaced book entries (no tombstone or deletion filter). Impact: Deletions and replaced Ids can only be detected with periodic full syncs, or by probing known Ids one at a time with GET /book-entries.info (N+1; whether a replaced or deleted Id returns '404 Not Found' is undocumented). Derived
  • Webhooks are mentioned ('adding new webhook types' in Guide: Changes & versioning), but no webhook documentation was provided. Impact: It is unknown whether push notifications for changes or deletions exist; the design assumes polling. To confirm
  • Book entries and account-history lines carry no invoice/credit-note flag, and the docs do not tie the write-side Type (0/1) to a daybook type. The only documented read-side discriminator is TransactionType on invoices.sales.outstanding.list (open sales items only). Impact: D1 makes the classification mandatory: both sibling daybook types are always scanned and each entry is classified by the partner-line sign (TransactionType overrides it for open sales items). The rule is treated as an assumption until Horus confirms it or exposes the document Type on read, and it doubles the book-entries.list calls when credit-note daybooks exist. Assumption
  • Line quantity, unit price, unit of measure, delivery date, payment term, payment method and withholding tax are not exposed on read. Impact: quantity=1, unit_price=untaxed_amount, all other fields null; the line detail of the original invoice is lost. Derived
  • Cash-discount terms (CompanyDetail.DiscountDetail: DiscountDate, DiscountAmount, DiscountRate) and the customer reminder level (ReminderLevel on customers.outstanding) are readable but have no Chift invoice field. Impact: This information is dropped; worth mentioning to clients doing credit control. Documented
  • The per-line tax amount is not stored on base lines (VAT is booked on separate VAT account lines), which line types carry VatDetail is undocumented, there is no VAT-code endpoint, the accepted-combination files were not provided, and VatDetail has no TaxKind. Impact: lines[].tax_amount must be allocated or computed, and tax_rate may have to be read from the VAT line. tax_code uses the canonical synthetic grammar (D2) and is null whenever the VatDetail components match zero or several catalogue entries (several purchase codes differing only by TaxKind, or TaxCondition '4 = Article'). To confirm
  • Analytics are stored as amounts per cost line (up to 8 schemas), while Chift expects whole-number percentages per plan. Impact: Rounding loss: percentages are approximations of the stored amounts. Derived
  • On the purchase side there is no open-invoice endpoint with linked payments. invoices.sales.outstanding.list covers only open sales items, and the docs do not say whether fully paid invoices appear in it. Impact: Payments and paid status must be rebuilt from MatchNo matching on the partner accounts, using an extra wide-period account-histories.list call. dedicated_amount is approximate in many-to-many matches, and 'paid' means 'fully matched', which includes offsets by credit notes or miscellaneous operations. Derived
  • invoices.sales.outstanding.list, customers.outstanding and suppliers.outstanding accept only FolderId plus CompanyIds (and ConfirmedOnly on the sales list): no date, daybook or ModifiedAfter filter. Impact: Each call returns every open item of the folder, whatever the Chift date range. Cache it per folder, or rely only on the partner-line MatchDetail from account-histories.list. Documented
  • The Horus status model is Confirmed / unconfirmed plus matching. There is no cancelled state and no approval workflow; only an optional Verified flag exists. Impact: status is limited to draft/posted/paid. approval_status is mostly 'unknown', and the approval_status filter can only be emulated or rejected. Derived
  • Documents are only downloadable as raw bytes; annexes cannot be discovered from the book entry. Impact: attachments_info.status can only be 'yes_to_request' or 'no', and annexes may be missed by the attachments route. Derived
  • Documents still being processed by Horus (documents.new, Status WaitingConfirmation, BeingProcessed and so on) are not book entries yet. Impact: Invoices sent to Horus as files but not yet booked do not appear in this route. Derived
  • It is unknown whether matching a payment updates the LastModifiedDate of the invoice entry or of its partner line. The ModifiedAfter description on book-entries.list mentions 'account-histories'. Impact: An updated_after sync may miss payment-status changes; the connector may need a separate open-items refresh. To confirm
  • Sideloading exists only on 4 endpoints, and book-entries.info is not one of them. The documented relationships are companies, daybooks and folders; including AccountHistories or Document is not documented, and the example shows a reduced Companies object. Impact: Lines cannot be assumed to arrive with headers in one call, so the separate account-histories.list call stays, and sideloaded partners may need a companies.list fallback. A single-invoice read through book-entries.info needs its own companies.info call for the partner. Documented
  • For foreign-currency entries, the docs do not say in which currency Debit/Credit and CompanyDetail amounts are expressed, nor the direction of CurrencyRate. D4 assumes folder-currency EUR everywhere; invoice-currency amounts exist on read only as TotalCurrencyAmount / OpenCurrencyAmount on invoices.sales.outstanding.list. Impact: Foreign-currency purchases and closed sales invoices are returned in EUR with currency = 'EUR' (the original currency is not visible in this route); only open foreign-currency sales invoices are returned in their own currency, with lines scaled by a derived ratio. accounting_info.main_currency_total is the EUR partner-line amount. If the assumption is wrong, amounts are wrong for non-EUR documents. Assumption
  • The invoice header Comment carries both reference and customer_memo under the D3 convention ('{reference} | {customer_memo}'), and where that Comment surfaces on read is undocumented (partner-line Comment, RemarkCompany or the sales Remark). Impact: reference and customer_memo are split from the first non-empty candidate (assumption). Comments typed in Horus Office without the convention land entirely in reference, and a reference containing ' | ' cannot round-trip. Assumption
  • Free cost schemas (CostSectionType 900) are excluded from Chift analytics in v1 (D8). Impact: Their cost lines are omitted from analytic_distribution with a connector warning, so the analytic picture of such folders is incomplete. Derived
  • The shared partner mapper (D9) returns is_company = true for every partner and ignores beneficiary data, because Horus stores no individual-versus-company flag. Impact: Private persons and beneficiaries read as companies, and first_name/last_name are always null: disclosed as data loss. Derived

items[].lines[].tax_codePOST /account-histories.list → VatDetail.VatRate, SaleTaxOrigin / PurchaseTaxOrigin, TaxType, TaxCountryCode, TaxCondition, TaxConditionValue → connector VAT catalogue (built from the Horus accepted-combination files, not provided)CompositeD2 canonical synthetic id, the same grammar as GET vat-codes and Invoice creation. Sales: 'S-{TaxRate}-{TaxOrigin}-{TaxType}', plus '-{TaxCountryCode}' only when TaxOrigin = 13 (OSS); example 'S-21-0-0'. Purchases: 'P-{TaxRate}-{TaxOrigin}-{TaxType}-{TaxKind}-{TaxCondition}-{TaxConditionValue}', with 'x' for an unused component; example 'P-21-2-0-0-1-true'. Read rule: build the key from VatDetail (VatRate, SaleTaxOrigin or PurchaseTaxOrigin, TaxType, TaxCountryCode, TaxCondition, TaxConditionValue) and return the catalogue id only if exactly one catalogue entry matches. VatDetail has NO TaxKind, so when several purchase entries match (differing only by TaxKind), or none does (for example read-side TaxCondition '4 = Article', which the write enum lacks), return tax_code = null and keep tax_rate / tax_amount. Never guess, and never return Horus VatCode as the Chift tax_code. Assumption: the catalogue depends on the accepted-combination files, which were not provided.AssumptionPOST /account-histories.list → VatDetail: 'VatRate', 'VatCode', 'TaxType: number|null // 0 = National, 1 = European, 2 = International', 'TaxCountryCode: string|null // Used for OSS sales', 'TaxCondition: number|null // *NULL* = ignore, 0 = Country, 1 = Deductibility, 2 = Investments, 3 = Percentage, 4 = Article', 'SaleTaxOrigin', 'PurchaseTaxOrigin' (no TaxKind); POST /invoices.purchases.new → InvoiceLines[].TaxKind: '0: MERCH (Merchandises) - 1: GS (Goods and services) - 2: INVEST (Investments)'; Guide: Posting sales & purchase invoices: 'Only some combinations are valid.
