Detailed open questions (14)
  • Horus What do cost-schemas.list and cost-sections.list return for a folder where WithAnalytics = false: an empty array, 400 or 403? Decides whether the connector must pre-check WithAnalytics and how errors are mapped.
  • Horus Does MasterCostSchema mean a hierarchical parent (sub-dimension) or another dependency? Does cost-sections.list return child sections (CostSections) at top level, and can master sections be used in CostLines? Needed to fill parent_id correctly and to avoid listing accounts that cannot be posted to.
  • Horus Guide: Data model says SearchKey is "unique within the folder", but invoices.*.new Costs[].Value is "The search key of an existing cost section, linked to this cost schema". Is a cost section SearchKey unique per folder or only per schema? Can accountants edit the SearchKeys of schemas and sections? Posting references schemas and sections by SearchKey. With GUID ids (D8) posting stays unambiguous, but the answer decides how far code can be trusted alone, and edited SearchKeys would make the D8 cache stale.
  • Horus Is CostHistories an array (the page text says 'a list of all cost histories'; the schema says object)? Does cost-histories.list include unconfirmed, Budget (900) and CostMiscOperation (700) daybook lines? Are there volume limits? Required before computing balance/credit/debit by aggregation.
  • Horus Is CostHistories.Balance signed (Debit - Credit, negative for credit rows) or unsigned? account-balance.list uses an unsigned Balance plus IsDebitBalance. The only example is a debit row (Debit 41.31, Credit null, Balance 41.31). Without the sign rule, summed balances may be wrong.
  • Horus What do IsLocked and IsDefault mean on a cost section, and Mandatory on a cost schema? Does IsLocked=true mean the section cannot be used in CostLines? Is IsDefault the section applied when a Mandatory schema gets no value? Does posting fail when a Mandatory schema gets no value, including a Free-type schema that Chift does not fill in v1 (D8)? Decides whether locked sections should be exposed as active=false, whether write routes must always send a value for mandatory schemas, and whether a Mandatory Free schema blocks Chift writes for that folder.
  • Horus Is the base currency of a Horus folder always EUR, and is there a field that exposes it? Are cost-histories amounts in that folder currency? To fill currency from a documented source instead of the D4 folder-currency assumption.
  • Horus The reference documents cost-schemas.info and cost-sections.info as POST with a body Id, while the Making requests guide says .info reads use GET. Which is correct? The connector does not call them (D8 cache only), but a correct description matters for any later single-object use and for the docs.
  • Horus Are there rate limits or concurrency limits (the N+1 calls could run in parallel)? No rate-limit documentation is provided.
  • Horus cost-schemas.list and cost-sections.list have no ModifiedAfter filter, and no webhook documentation was provided. Is there any change signal (filter, event or version stamp) for cost schemas and sections? The D8 cache feeds both the analytics reads and the write-route validation (inactive → 422). A reliable refresh trigger avoids stale ids and false rejections.
  • Chift D8 excludes Horus Free-type schemas (CostSectionType 900) from Chift analytics in v1: not listed by analytic-plans/accounts, not accepted on write, omitted on read with a warning. Is that acceptable for v1, and should a later version expose them (for example as plans whose values are free text rather than analytic_account ids)? AnalyticPlanItem has no type field and analytic_account expects an id, so Free values do not fit the current model. Clients that analyse by a Free dimension lose it.
  • Chift For balance/credit/debit when the target has no native balance per analytic account: return the default 0, null, or a value computed over the current fiscal year? Confirmed-only or all entries? What sign convention does Chift expect for balance (the spec gives no description)? The route has no date parameters, and a default 0 is indistinguishable from a real zero balance.
  • Chift For a folder without the analytics module: return an empty page or an explicit error? Consistency across connectors.
  • Chift D8 fixes analytic_plan = cost schema GUID and analytic_account = cost section GUID. Can Chift confirm this suits consumers that pass the ids back (analytic_distribution.analytic_plan / analytic_account, MiscellaneousOperationLine.analytic_account), given that the connector translates them into Horus SchemaSearchKey / Value from the per-folder cache? The id convention must be consistent across the analytics, invoice and misc-operation routes, and a GUID is not human-readable (the plan SearchKey has no slot in AnalyticPlanItem).
Outstanding items (customers and suppliers)Supported with workaroundGET /consumers/{consumer_id}/accounting/outstandingsFolder-level open items exist on both sides, but the client and supplier paths differ; supplier open amounts are computed from matching groups.

Horus has folder-level open-item lists, but the two sides differ. Client items come from the open sales invoices list (with a native open amount), joined with customer ledger lines for account and matching details. Supplier items come from supplier ledger lines, with the open amount computed from matching groups. Journal ids and types come from cached daybooks, and pagination is emulated.

Detailed analysis

Horus documents folder-level open-item endpoints for both sides (Guide: Reading accounting data: 'Open items of customers | customers.outstanding', 'Open items of suppliers | suppliers.outstanding'), so no N+1 loop over partners is needed if Filter.CompanyIds is omitted (derived: Guide: Making requests: 'omitted or null filters are ignored'; that the result is then complete and uncapped is A). For type=client, POST /invoices.sales.outstanding.list returns document-level open items with a native OpenAmount/OpenCurrencyAmount, a ConfirmedOnly filter, PaymentRemark and Daybook.Id, already in the receivable view (invoice +, payment −). It is joined on AccountHistory.Id with one POST /customers.outstanding call for the account number, MatchNo, rate and partner-line Comment; a sideloaded AccountHistory could replace that call (assumption). For type=supplier there is no purchase equivalent: POST /suppliers.outstanding returns ledger lines, so amount and open_amount are computed with the shared side-based sign rule (customer Debit − Credit, supplier Credit − Debit, positive = owed; D5). They are returned in EUR with rate 1 because ledger amounts are assumed to be in folder currency (D4, A). Every item takes journal_type from its daybook Type through the shared D6 table (cached POST /daybooks.list). reference is the left part of the header comment, read from the partner-line Comment, then RemarkCompany, then Remark (D3, A), and folder_id follows the single D7 rule. Horus has no paging, so Chift fetches everything, filters on Confirmed, sorts and slices. Main risks: undocumented sign/currency conventions and Partially-matched semantics, item ids that change when the source entry is updated, a single due date with no readable payment method, and an uncertain original_document for carried-forward (IsReport) lines.

Horus endpoints

  • PrimaryPOST /invoices.sales.outstanding.list type=client primary source. Returns open sale invoices / credit notes and their linked or standalone payments, with native OpenAmount/OpenCurrencyAmount, TotalAmount/TotalCurrencyAmount, DueDate, PaymentRemark, Remark, TransactionType, Daybook.Id, AccountHistory.Id, Company.Id and Confirmed. Its amounts are already in the receivable view (invoice +, payment −; D5). TotalCurrencyAmount/OpenCurrencyAmount are the only invoice-currency amounts available on read (D4). Remark is the last source of the D3 reference chain, and Daybook.Id feeds the D6 journal_type lookup. Filter.ConfirmedOnly implements unposted_allowed=false natively, and Filter.CompanyIds is omitted. Sideloading is supported, so AccountHistory/Daybook could come back in the same call (assumption: the sideloadable paths are not documented). (1 per Chift call (folder-level, CompanyIds omitted))
  • EnrichmentPOST /customers.outstanding type=client enrichment, joined on AccountHistories[].Id = Data[].AccountHistory.Id. Supplies AccountSearchKey (account_number), MatchDetail.MatchNo (matching_numbers), CurrencyCode/CurrencyRate, IsReport, DocumentNo, Comment (first source of the D3 reference chain) and Confirmed. It also supplies customer lines the sales list does not reference (reconciliation, A), and it is the single-call fallback primary for clients. For those ledger lines, amount = Debit − Credit and open_amount = line or match-group residual Σ(Debit − Credit) (customer side, D5), returned in EUR with rate 1 (D4). The call can be skipped if Horus confirms that AccountHistory can be sideloaded on the sales list with these fields. (1 per Chift call (folder-level, Filter.CompanyIds omitted))
  • PrimaryPOST /suppliers.outstanding type=supplier primary source. Returns open lines on supplier accounts: AccountSearchKey, DaybookSearchKey, DocumentNo, Date, DueDate, Debit, Credit, Confirmed, CurrencyCode, CurrencyRate, CompanyDetail, MatchDetail, BookEntry, IsReport, Comment, Memo. There is no open-amount field and no ConfirmedOnly filter. amount is therefore Credit − Debit and open_amount is computed with the supplier-side formula (D5). Amounts are returned in EUR with currency 'EUR' and rate 1, because ledger amounts are assumed to be in folder currency (D4, A). Confirmed is applied as a post-filter. Comment is the first source of the D3 reference chain. (1 per Chift call (folder-level, Filter.CompanyIds omitted))
  • LookupPOST /daybooks.list Cached lookup: Id -> {SearchKey, Type} and SearchKey -> Id. Supplies journal_id for supplier lines, which carry only DaybookSearchKey. Supplies journal_type for every item through the shared daybook Type → journal_type table (D6): client rows via Data[].Daybook.Id, all other lines via DaybookSearchKey. Also used for original_document, and to decide which lines follow the D3 invoice-comment split (Types 100/150/200/250). (cached reference data, 1 per folder (refresh once on cache miss))
  • Follow-upGET /account-histories.info Fallback when a sales-list row has no matching line in the customers.outstanding snapshot (race or scope difference): fetch AccountSearchKey, MatchDetail, CurrencyRate and Comment by AccountHistory.Id. The response has no Folder reference, so the D13 cross-folder guard runs through the book entry. The returned Data.BookEntry.Id must equal the referring sales-list row's BookEntry.Id (that row comes from the FolderId-scoped list). Otherwise GET /book-entries.info on that BookEntry.Id must show Data.Folder.Id = FolderId. See rejected_findings. (1 per unmatched item (N+1, expected rare))
  • OptionalGET /book-entries.info Optional per-entry enrichment through the BookEntry reference that outstanding lines carry (customers/suppliers.outstanding AccountHistories[].BookEntry.Id; sales list Data[].BookEntry.Id). Supplies: CompanyDetail.RemarkPayment (payment_communication for supplier purchase-daybook lines and client reconciliation lines, A); CompanyDetail.RemarkCompany (second source of the D3 reference chain, for lines whose partner-line Comment is empty, A); CompanyDetail.DueDate (due-date fallback, A, off by default); and EntryType/Daybook.Id (to classify the entry that carries an IsReport line, for original_document). Every response must show Data.Folder.Id = FolderId (D13 cross-folder guard); a mismatch returns Chift 400. Its Document reference adds nothing for this route: the outstanding lines already carry Document.Id and OutstandingItem has no attachment field. Preferred over book-entries.list because it needs no DaybookId or period, reads only the needed entries, and can be cached across requests by BookEntry.Id (an update generates a new book entry). Not needed for regular client rows, which have PaymentRemark and DueDate natively. (1 per distinct BookEntry.Id needing enrichment (N+1 on a cold cache; close to 0 once cached by BookEntry.Id))
  • AlternativePOST /book-entries.list Bulk alternative to GET /book-entries.info for the same optional enrichment: CompanyDetail.RemarkPayment (presumed payment communication, A) and CompanyDetail.RemarkCompany (D3 reference chain, A). Joined on Data[].Id = BookEntry.Id or on Data[].AccountHistories[].Id. It requires DaybookId and a period, so it takes 1 call per daybook/period group. It returns every entry of that daybook in the window, open or not, so one old open invoice stretches the window over years. Worth it only on a cold cache with many open lines concentrated in few daybooks over a short window. Sideloading is supported. (1 per distinct (DaybookId, period range) among returned supplier items (cold-cache bulk load only))
  • AlternativePOST /account-histories.list Alternative source if folder-level customers/suppliers.outstanding turn out to be capped or incomplete. Reads unmatched lines of the customer/supplier concentration accounts (NotMatchedOnly: true), with ConfirmedOnly, IncludeMatchDetail and IncludeCompanyDetail, but needs a period. Lines follow the same side-based sign rule (D5) and EUR rule (D4) as the main path. It is also the documented way to read open lines on beneficiary (employee) current accounts, which Chift does not need. (1 per Chift call (period from fiscal years), or 1 per partner with Filter.CompanyId)
  • LookupPOST /accounts.list ALT path lookup: resolve concentration account Ids (companies.list CustomerConcentrationAccount / SupplierConcentrationAccount) to SearchKey for Filter.AccountSearchKeys. IsMatchable narrows the set to matchable accounts. (cached reference data, 1 per folder (ALT only))
  • LookupPOST /fiscal-years.list ALT/optional lookup: fiscal-year bounds (StartDate/EndDate) for the period-bound account-histories.list and book-entries.list calls. Does NOT return IsClosed. (cached reference data, 1 per folder)
  • LookupGET /fiscal-years.info ALT only: read IsClosed for each fiscal year, to start account-histories.list at the first year that is not closed. Each response must show Data.Folder.Id = FolderId (D13 cross-folder guard); otherwise Chift 400. (1 per fiscal year (few), cached)
  • OptionalPOST /companies.list Optional uses: CompanyIds batches (IsCustomer / IsSupplier) if Horus says folder-level calls are capped; concentration account Ids for the ALT path; beneficiary identification (BeneficiaryInfo.BeneficiaryType '10: Employee', DefaultCurrentAccount); party-level payment-mode fallback (UsualCustomerPaymentMode / UsualSupplierPaymentMode). (1 per folder (cacheable))
  • LookupPOST /folders.list Resolve the Horus FolderId behind Chift folder_id under the shared D7 rule. Called with Mode 0, and only when folder_id is null and no folder selection is stored for the connection. A single returned folder is used; several -> Chift 400 'folder_id required'. (cached, at connection setup)
  • OptionalGET /invoices.sales.outstanding.info Not needed for the list. Returns one open sale invoice with its linked elements; could serve a per-item refresh. (1 per item (not used in the list flow))
  • OptionalPOST /matching Not called by this read route. It shows that the matchable unit is the account history line and that MatchNo is scoped per company, which is why AccountHistory.Id is used as the Chift id. (0 (used by Chift matching routes, not here))

Call sequence

  1. Resolve consumer_id to the stored Horus connection record: license api_url, access token, single-use refresh token and expiry. Refresh proactively before expiry. FolderId follows the shared D7 rule: folder_id when given. If folder_id is null, use the single folder selected at activation; if no selection is stored, use the single folder returned by POST /folders.list {Mode: 0}; otherwise return Chift 400 'folder_id required'. Send 'Content-Encoding: gzip' on every Horus call below (Guide: Making requests).
  2. [both types, cached] POST /daybooks.list {FolderId} -> maps Id -> {SearchKey, Type} and SearchKey -> Id, and Type -> journal_type through the shared D6 table. On a miss, refresh once (daybooks.update can change SearchKey).
  3. [type=client] POST /invoices.sales.outstanding.list {FolderId, Filter:{ConfirmedOnly: (unposted_allowed == 'false')}}, with CompanyIds omitted (not []). Option (assumption, pending Horus confirmation): add "Include": "AccountHistory,Daybook" and read AccountSearchKey, MatchDetail and CurrencyRate from Included.AccountHistories, which would make step 3a unnecessary.
  4. [type=client] POST /customers.outstanding {FolderId} (Filter omitted, or no CompanyIds) -> index Data.AccountHistories[] by Id (AccountSearchKey, MatchDetail.MatchNo, CurrencyCode, CurrencyRate, IsReport, DocumentNo, Comment, Confirmed, DaybookSearchKey). Run in parallel with 2a.
  5. [type=client] Clean and join. (i) Drop top-level rows whose AccountHistory.Id appears in another row's LinkedElements[] (already netted into that invoice's OpenAmount). (ii) Drop rows with OpenAmount == 0 and log them (to confirm). (iii) Join 2a.Data[].AccountHistory.Id = 3a.AccountHistories[].Id. Rows missing from 3a -> GET /account-histories.info?Id=... (rare N+1). account-histories.info has no Folder field, so the D13 guard runs through the book entry: accept the line only if its Data.BookEntry.Id equals the row's BookEntry.Id, or else GET /book-entries.info on that BookEntry.Id shows Data.Folder.Id = FolderId. A proven mismatch -> Chift 400; an unverifiable line (BookEntry null) -> skip the item and log. (iv) Reconcile (assumption): emit customers.outstanding lines that no sales-list row references (top-level or LinkedElements) and log them. They use the customer-side ledger rule: amount = Debit − Credit, open_amount = the line's amount or match-group residual Σ(Debit − Credit) (D5), in EUR with rate 1 (D4). Keep AccountType 100, and apply Confirmed==true when unposted_allowed=='false'. (v) Control check (derived): compare Σ OpenAmount of the kept sales-list rows (EUR view) plus the reconciliation residuals with SumDebit − SumCredit of 3a (customer-side sign, D5), and log any gap.
  6. [type=supplier] POST /suppliers.outstanding {FolderId} (Filter omitted, or no CompanyIds) -> Data.AccountHistories[].
  7. [type=supplier] Post-filters: keep Confirmed == true when unposted_allowed == 'false'; keep AccountType 200 (defensive, since the docs example is a placeholder); drop MatchDetail.MatchStatus 900 'Totally' (defensive: the guide says outstanding items are lines not matched yet). amount = Credit − Debit (supplier side, D5). A line with MatchNo null gets open_amount = amount. If lines with a non-null MatchNo come back ('0: Partially', A): group by (CompanyDetail.Company.Id, MatchNo), compute residual = Σ(Credit − Debit) (D5), allocate it to the positive (invoice-side) lines oldest DueDate first, and drop fully consumed payment lines.
  8. [type=supplier] journal_id from the daybooks cache (DaybookSearchKey); journal_type from its Type via the shared D6 table. Amounts are returned in EUR with currency 'EUR' and currency_exchange_rate 1, whatever CurrencyCode says (D4, A); a foreign CurrencyCode is logged. Optional payment_communication for lines in a '100: Purchase' or '150: PurchaseCreditNote' daybook: CompanyDetail.RemarkPayment of the line's book entry (assumption), read in step 4c (GET /book-entries.info per distinct BookEntry.Id by default; POST /book-entries.list per daybook/period as the bulk alternative).
  9. [both, optional enrichment through BookEntry.Id] Collect the distinct BookEntry.Id values of: (i) supplier lines in a purchase daybook (RemarkPayment -> payment_communication, A); (ii) client reconciliation lines from 4a(iv), which have no PaymentRemark (same); (iii) IsReport=true lines on either side (EntryType, Daybook.Id -> original_document, A); (iv) only if the fallback is enabled, invoice-type lines with DueDate null (CompanyDetail.DueDate, A); (v) lines on either side whose partner-line Comment is null or empty (CompanyDetail.RemarkCompany -> reference, second step of the D3 chain, A). Skip Ids already in a cross-request cache keyed by BookEntry.Id (derived: an update generates a new book entry, so an invoice update cannot change what a cached Id returned). Call GET /book-entries.info?Id=... for the remaining Ids, with bounded concurrency and the token/500 handling of step 6. A 400/404 (entry replaced since the snapshot) leaves the enrichment null without failing the request. Cross-folder guard (D13): every response must have Data.Folder.Id = FolderId, otherwise return Chift 400. Use an entry's RemarkPayment, RemarkCompany or DueDate only if its CompanyDetail.Company.Id equals the line's partner_id. Cost rule (derived): .info costs 1 small call per distinct entry and nothing on a warm cache. POST /book-entries.list {FolderId, DaybookId, Start/End = min/max AccountingMonth/AccountingYear of the group} costs 1 call per daybook but returns every entry of that window, matched or not. Use .list for a daybook group only on a cold cache with many distinct entries and a short window; tune the threshold on a test license, since rate limits are undocumented (assumption).
  10. [both] Map to OutstandingItem. journal_type comes from the daybook Type via the shared D6 table. amount/open_amount use the side-based sign rule (D5). Each item has one currency view: EUR with rate 1 wherever invoice-currency amounts are missing (D4). reference = the first non-empty value of partner-line Comment -> RemarkCompany -> Remark, split on the first ' | ' for invoice-daybook lines (D3). Sort deterministically (date asc, then id asc), set total = item count, return slice [(page-1)*size, page*size). Cache the full result for a short TTL keyed by (consumer, folder, type, unposted_allowed).
  11. [errors, Guide: Responses & errors] Horus 401 -> refresh the token once and retry; if it fails again, return a Chift auth error asking the user to reconnect. Horus 400 -> no retry; log Details and return Chift 400 (ChiftError) carrying Horus Details (e.g. unknown FolderId). Horus 403 -> Chift 400 'folder not accessible with this connection' (D7). Horus 404 -> Chift 400 with an explicit error_code (not found), because the Chift contract declares only 400 and 422. A cross-folder mismatch found by the D13 guard -> Chift 400. Horus 500 -> retry with exponential backoff, then a Chift error. An invalid type or unposted_allowed is rejected with Chift 422 before any Horus call.
  12. ALT (only if Horus says folder-level outstanding calls are capped or incomplete). Step 1: POST /fiscal-years.list {IncludeFuture: true}, plus GET /fiscal-years.info per year to read IsClosed (each response checked for Data.Folder.Id = FolderId, D13). Step 2: POST /accounts.list to resolve the concentration accounts (from companies.list) to search keys. Step 3: POST /account-histories.list {FolderId, Start/End, Filter:{NotMatchedOnly: true, ConfirmedOnly: (unposted_allowed=='false'), IncludeCompanyDetail: true, IncludeMatchDetail: true, AccountSearchKeys: [concentration account keys]}}. Keep AccountType 100 or 200, apply the side-based sign rule (D5: Debit − Credit for 100, Credit − Debit for 200) and return amounts in EUR (D4). To avoid double counting, EITHER start at the first year that is not closed and keep IncludeReport at its default (true), OR read the full history with IncludeReport: false (assumption). Second option: POST /companies.list {IsCustomer|IsSupplier} and batch CompanyIds into customers/suppliers.outstanding.

Considerations and limitations

  • No pagination: neither the reference (72 endpoints) nor the Making requests / Responses & errors guides define page, size, offset or total. Every Chift page means fetching the folder's whole open-item set and slicing it in Chift. Rate limits and timeouts are not documented anywhere provided. Impact: Higher latency and timeout risk for large folders. Mitigate with gzip ('highly recommended to reduce payload sizes') and a short TTL cache so pages stay consistent. Derived
  • There is no purchase-side equivalent of invoices.sales.outstanding.list (both guide outstanding tables list only invoices.sales.outstanding.list/.info). Supplier amount and open_amount must be rebuilt from Debit/Credit and MatchDetail with the supplier-side sign rule (Credit − Debit, D5). Impact: Supplier open amounts are an emulation. If partially matched lines are returned, multi-invoice groups need an allocation rule that may not match Horus's screens. Clients and suppliers are calculated differently. Derived
  • For clients, the sales list lacks account number, MatchNo and currency rate, so it is joined with customers.outstanding on AccountHistory.Id. The two calls are separate snapshots, not one atomic read. Sideloading AccountHistory might remove the second call (assumption). Impact: Two calls per request, with possible mismatches if data changes between them (handled by the account-histories.info fallback). Derived
  • Horus has one DueDate per document and no instalment structure; the invoice PaymentMode is write-only. Impact: due_dates has at most one element. payment_method and payment_method_id are null, or at best filled with the party's usual payment mode. Derived
  • original_document can only be guessed from IsReport lines. IsReport is undefined in the reference and the guides. GET /book-entries.info on the line's BookEntry.Id gives the EntryType of the entry that carries it (e.g. '910: OpeningMO'), which tells whether the line sits in an opening entry, but neither the line nor its book entry links to the prior-year document. Impact: original_document.id is always null. The other original_document fields are filled, partly filled or null depending on EntryType and on Horus's answer about what report lines keep, at the cost of one cached book-entries.info call per distinct carrier entry. To confirm
  • No documented sign convention: Debit/Credit are number|null and TotalAmount is signed only in an example. The outstanding examples look like placeholders. By general accounting knowledge (not a Horus source), a sale debits the customer account, yet the customers.outstanding example shows a Credit on a 'VEN' line, and the suppliers.outstanding example shows a Debit and AccountType 100 on a '440000 Fournisseurs' / 'ACH' line. The connector applies one side-based rule on every path (D5): customer lines Debit − Credit, supplier lines Credit − Debit, positive = owed; sales-list rows are already in the receivable view. Impact: The sign of amount/open_amount (especially for suppliers, credit notes and payments) must be validated on a test license or test folder before go-live. Horus has no separate sandbox: 'Requests run against the license's real database.' To confirm
  • Foreign-currency semantics are unclear. Account history lines carry CurrencyCode/CurrencyRate but no foreign-currency amount, and the rate direction is undocumented. Under D4 (assumption), ledger Debit/Credit are taken as folder-currency (EUR) amounts and are never paired with a foreign currency code. Impact: Supplier items and client reconciliation/fallback items of foreign-currency documents are returned in EUR, with currency 'EUR' and currency_exchange_rate 1, so the document currency and its amounts are lost for them. Client sales-list rows keep the invoice currency through TotalCurrencyAmount/OpenCurrencyAmount. Assumption
  • Beneficiary (employee) open items are not exposed by customers/suppliers.outstanding: AccountType has no beneficiary value, and matching next-number exists only for customer, supplier and account. They can be read as a workaround via account-histories.list {CompanyId, NotMatchedOnly: true} on the beneficiary's current account (BeneficiaryInfo.DefaultCurrentAccount -> account search key). Impact: Out of scope for this route, since Chift OutstandingType is only client|supplier, even though partner_id mentions employees. A period-bound workaround exists if Chift ever needs it. Derived
  • MatchNo is scoped per company (customer/supplier) or per general account, not per folder. Impact: matching_numbers values are not unique across partners. Chift consumers must read them with partner_id, which conflicts with Chift MatchingOut's 'Unique number of the matching operation'. Derived
  • Some Horus information has no Chift field: ReminderLevel (customers only), PaymentStatus, LastPaymentDate, LinkedElements detail, VatDetail, and the cash discount (book-entries.list / book-entries.info CompanyDetail.DiscountDetail DiscountDate/DiscountAmount/DiscountRate; written via invoices.*.new Discount). Impact: Credit-control data such as the reminder level and early-payment discount terms is lost in the unified model. Documented
  • Unconfirmed entries can carry number 0 ('It can be 0 when the invoice is not confirmed'; 'it can be 0 while the entry is not confirmed'). Impact: number is null for unposted items when unposted_allowed=true. Documented
  • Item ids are only as stable as the source entry. Updating an invoice or operation (invoices.*.update, book-entries.*.update) generates a new book entry with a new Id. Its account history lines, and so the Chift outstanding id, presumably change too. Impact: An outstanding id stored by a Chift consumer can disappear before it is used for matching. Consumers must re-read before matching; fallback business key = journal_id + number + partner_id. Derived
  • Entries pushed through the API, including invoices and payments Chift creates, are unconfirmed by default ('Entries pushed through the API are unconfirmed by default ( Confirmed: false )'). Impact: Under the proposed default (D12: 'posted' → Confirmed=true), entries Chift pushes as posted are confirmed and appear with unposted_allowed=false. Drafts, and every entry of a consumer that uses the 'always push unconfirmed' option, stay unconfirmed and appear only with unposted_allowed=true until the accountant confirms them in Horus Office. Explain this to clients who choose accountant review (derived). Documented
  • The outstanding endpoints cannot be read incrementally. Their Filter has only CompanyIds, IncludeVatDetail and UseAlternateHeading; there is no ModifiedAfter, only a per-line LastModifiedDate. Impact: Every cache miss reloads the full set. On the ALT path account-histories.list has ModifiedAfter, but a delta cannot reliably remove lines that were replaced (new Ids) or matched, so a full reload stays the v1 rule. Documented
  • customers.outstanding and suppliers.outstanding do not support sideloading (the Sideloading guide lists only book-entries.list, account-histories.list, companies.list and invoices.sales.outstanding.list). Book-entry data such as RemarkPayment, RemarkCompany or EntryType therefore costs separate calls: GET /book-entries.info per distinct BookEntry.Id, or POST /book-entries.list per daybook and period. Impact: Supplier payment_communication, the RemarkCompany step of the reference chain (D3) and original_document together cost up to one call per distinct book entry on a cold cache. Mitigated by a cross-request cache keyed by BookEntry.Id and by enriching only purchase-daybook, client reconciliation, IsReport and empty-Comment lines. All three Chift fields are optional, so the enrichment can be switched off if latency matters. Derived
  • journal_type is the daybook's type through the shared D6 table, not the document's nature. A credit note posted in a '200: Sale' or '100: Purchase' daybook is reported as customer_invoice / supplier_invoice. The Horus TransactionType of client rows ('1: Sale credit note') has no Chift field. Impact: Consumers must use the sign of amount/open_amount (D5), not journal_type, to tell credit notes from invoices. In exchange, journal_type stays consistent with Get journals for the same journal_id. Derived
  • reference relies on the D3 header-comment convention and on an assumed read location (partner-line Comment → RemarkCompany → Remark), none of which Horus documents as the invoice header Comment. Impact: reference may be null or come from the wrong field until Horus confirms. Comments typed outside Chift that contain ' | ' are cut to their left part. customer_memo has no OutstandingItem field and is dropped. Assumption
  • Full attribute-level mapping in section 5 (Outstanding items (customers and suppliers)). Derived
Detailed open questions (24)
  • Horus If Filter.CompanyIds is omitted, do customers.outstanding, suppliers.outstanding and invoices.sales.outstanding.list return the open lines of every customer/supplier in the folder? Is there a size cap or hidden pagination, and what does an explicit empty array [] do? Decides between 1 folder-level call and per-company batching. The Making requests guide says 'omitted or null filters are ignored', but the PAGE TEXT and the matching guide say 'a customer's' (singular), while the Reading accounting data guide says 'Open items of customers'.
  • Horus Do customers/suppliers.outstanding return lines with MatchStatus '0: Partially', and if so every line of the partial group, including payments? Does account-histories.list NotMatchedOnly also exclude partially matched lines? The guide defines outstanding items as 'lines that are not matched yet' but does not define Partially. This decides whether supplier open_amount needs group netting.
  • Horus What is the sign convention of Debit/Credit on outstanding lines? The connector assumes (D5) that Debit − Credit on customer lines (AccountType 100) and Credit − Debit on supplier lines (AccountType 200) give the amount owed. Is that correct? The examples look like placeholders (Credit on a VEN customer line; Debit and AccountType 100 on a 440000 supplier line), and amount/open_amount depend on the answer.
  • Horus Direction and meaning of CurrencyRate; currency of CompanyDetail amounts. Are account-history Debit/Credit and book-entry CompanyDetail amounts always in the folder currency (EUR)? Is CurrencyRate EUR per unit of CurrencyCode, or units per EUR? Until this is confirmed, D4 returns ledger-line amounts of foreign-currency documents in EUR with currency 'EUR' and rate 1. A confirmed rule could restore the document currency for supplier items.
  • Horus What does IsReport mean exactly? Does an IsReport=true line keep the original DocumentNo, Date, DueDate and Daybook, which EntryType does its book entry have (e.g. '910: OpeningMO'), and is there any link to the prior-year account history? Decides whether original_document can be filled or must be null. book-entries.info gives the carrier entry's EntryType, but not what the line keeps.
  • Horus Does invoices.sales.outstanding.list include invoices carried forward from previous fiscal years and every customer line that customers.outstanding returns? Why is a linked payment also returned as a top-level row, and can rows with PaymentStatus '800: Paid' or OpenAmount 0 occur? Completeness, deduplication and the reconciliation step for client items.
  • Horus What does MatchStatus 0 ('Partially') mean when MatchNo is null? Both outstanding examples show this combination, and it affects open_amount and matching_numbers.
  • Horus On invoices.sales.outstanding.list, can Include contain 'AccountHistory' and 'Daybook'? Do Included.AccountHistories carry AccountSearchKey, MatchDetail and CurrencyRate, and Included.Daybooks carry Type? On account-histories.list, can Include 'BookEntry' return CompanyDetail.RemarkPayment? The Sideloading guide documents the syntax and the supported endpoints but not the paths or fields. A yes would reduce the client side to one call and, on the ALT path, deliver RemarkPayment without per-entry book-entries.info calls (customers/suppliers.outstanding are not sideloadable, so the main path needs book-entries.info or book-entries.list).
  • Horus On book-entries.info / book-entries.list, does CompanyDetail.RemarkPayment contain the PaymentReferences written through invoices.*.new, CompanyDetail.DueDate the invoice DueDate, and RemarkCompany the invoice-level Comment? These CompanyDetail fields have no description (the book-entries.info example shows them all null). RemarkPayment is the only candidate for supplier payment_communication, DueDate would serve as a due_date fallback, and RemarkCompany is the second source of the D3 reference chain.
  • Horus Where does the invoice header Comment surface on read (partner-line Comment, RemarkCompany, Remark)? reference is read through the D3 chain: partner-line Comment, then RemarkCompany, then Remark. The RemarkCompany step costs a book-entries.info call per entry, and none of the three fields is documented as the header Comment.
  • Horus Can a credit note (Type 1) be posted into a 200/100 daybook, and an invoice into 250/150? journal_type describes the daybook (D6). If credit notes can sit in invoice daybooks, consumers must rely on the sign (D5) to identify them, and the shared D1 rule depends on the same answer.
  • Horus Can book-entries.info take several Ids in one call, or can sideloading of BookEntry be enabled on customers/suppliers.outstanding (the Sideloading guide invites requests for other endpoints)? Which status does book-entries.info return for an entry replaced by an update? Supplier enrichment otherwise costs one call per distinct book entry on a cold cache, and the 400/404 handling of replaced entries depends on the answer.
  • Horus Can GET /account-histories.info return the line's Folder reference, as book-entries.info and fiscal-years.info do? Its response has no Folder field, so the D13 cross-folder guard must go through the line's BookEntry, which can cost an extra book-entries.info call. Low priority (rare fallback path).
  • Horus Does a line created with a pre-assigned MatchNo, with no counterpart yet, still appear in customers/suppliers.outstanding? If it counts as matched, open invoices created that way would silently disappear from the outstanding list.
  • Horus When an invoice/operation is updated and a new book entry is generated, do its account history lines get new Ids? Does POST /matching keep line Ids unchanged? The Chift outstanding id is the account history Id, so its stability matters for matching.
  • Horus Is there, or will there be, a purchase-side open-invoice endpoint with OpenAmount? It would remove the supplier open-amount emulation.
  • Horus For invoices.sales.outstanding.info, is the 'Id' query parameter the FolderId the guide mentions? The guide says it 'requires the FolderId', but the schema has no parameter with that name. Low priority (not used in the list flow).
  • Horus What rate limits, request timeouts and maximum response sizes apply? Is 'Content-Encoding: gzip' on the request really what triggers gzipped responses? The route fetches the full open-item set on every cache miss.
  • Chift What sign convention is expected for amount/open_amount (credit notes, payments, supplier invoices)? The connector proposes D5: positive = owed (receivable for clients, payable for suppliers), with credit notes and payments negative. The contract only says 'Total amount' and 'Open amount'.
  • Chift Should unallocated payments and credit balances be returned as outstanding items? Horus returns them (TransactionType 2 rows), and they affect totals.
  • Chift Is due_dates[].amount the scheduled amount or the remaining amount per due date? The connector returns the item currency only where Horus gives invoice-currency amounts (client sales-list rows) and EUR with rate 1 otherwise (D4): is that acceptable? Is the party's usual payment mode acceptable as a payment_method fallback? Horus gives a single due date, both EUR and currency amounts on the client side only, and no invoice-level payment method on read.
  • Chift Is a partner-scoped matching number acceptable in matching_numbers, or should the connector prefix it (e.g. with the partner)? Horus MatchNo is unique per company/account only, while Chift MatchingOut describes matching_number as 'Unique'.
  • Chift Will consumers pass outstanding item ids (Horus account history lines) to POST /accounting/matching, or invoice/journal entry ids? MatchingIn.entries says entries are 'typically journal entry id, invoice id or financial entry id', while Horus matching takes account history line ids. The matching connector must resolve book entry ids to partner lines if consumers send entry ids.
  • Chift Should employee items be returned under type=supplier for connectors where that is possible? OutstandingType has only client/supplier, while partner_id mentions employees.

5Attribute-level mapping (Task B)

Each table lists every Chift attribute and query parameter of the route: the request side, and what the connector returns. Error responses follow the same pattern on all three routes: see the error rows of the client creation table and the error translation table in section 7. Use the filter to find a field or show only one mapping type. The evidence column quotes the Horus documentation and can be hidden with the button in the sidebar.

Client creation Supported with workaround

POST /consumers/{consumer_id}/accounting/clients

A Chift client is a Horus company (third party) with IsCustomer = true, created with companies.new in the folder. Horus requires a search key, a VAT-liability flag, a language and a country that Chift does not always send, so the connector derives them. Creation returns only an id, so the connector reads the company back (company details, IBANs and the receivable account) to build the Chift response. Because one Horus company can be both customer and supplier, force_merge maps to flagging an existing supplier as customer.

Horus fields with no Chift source, and how the connector fills them

Horus fieldFill ruleBasis
POST /companies.new → SearchKey *Generate from name: uppercase, remove accents, keep A-Z0-9, truncate (the limit is unknown; a conservative 10-15 characters is an assumption). Search keys are 'unique within the folder' (documented, Guide: Data model), so check the candidate is free in that FolderId via companies.list Filter.SearchKey (contains) with an exact post-filter, appending 2, 3… on collision. Still to confirm: max length, charset, case sensitivity, and whether Horus generates a key when it is empty. A race between check and create can collide; regenerate only if the Horus 400 points to a SearchKey conflict. Users may expect their own coding scheme (could be a connector setting). error.error_code (ChiftError, HTTP 400/422)Horus HTTP status + error body ErrorTypeTransformChift-side validation failures → 422 before any Horus call (ERROR_INVALID_VAT_FORMAT for a malformed vat; ERROR_INVALID_FIELD_FORMAT_COMPANY_ID only if company_id is rejected rather than ignored). Call sequence

  1. Connection and conventions. consumer_id → stored connection record for one Horus license (api_url, access_token, refresh_token, expiry; Guide: Authentication overview, 'one record per connected license'). If expiry is a few minutes away, refresh through POST {api_url}/oauth2/access_token (grant_type=refresh_token) under a per-connection lock, and persist the new pair before using it, because refresh tokens are single-use. Every call: HTTPS to api_url, Authorization: Bearer, Content-Type: application/json, optional gzip. .info = GET with ?Id=; .list/.new/.update = POST with a JSON body. Deserialise responses tolerantly (ignore unknown properties, never rely on property order).
  2. Resolve the folder (D7). folder_id given → FolderId; reject with 400 if it is not among the folders selected at Chift activation (Chift FolderItem.selected: "Requests targeting a folder that was not selected will be rejected"). folder_id absent → if exactly one folder was selected at activation, use it; if no selection is stored, POST /folders.list {Mode:0} (cached) and use the folder if exactly one is returned; otherwise (several selected or visible folders) reject with 400 'folder_id required'. GET /folders.info?Id=FolderId (cached) gives the Language and CountryCode defaults. Horus 403 → Chift 400 'folder not accessible with this connection'.
  3. Local validation and transformation (Chift 422 before any Horus call). Pick one address (main > invoice > delivery). Build Address = street + ' ' + number (+ '/' + box). Map country → uppercase alpha-2 CountryCode, checked against the cached countries.list. Map language → LanguageCode (FR/NL/DE/EN/ES/PT, otherwise the folder Language, possibly 'XX': the D9 write-side fallback shared by every write route). Normalise vat (uppercase, strip spaces/dots/dashes); if malformed → 422 ERROR_INVALID_VAT_FORMAT. IsVatLiable = vat present. Strip and uppercase the IBAN(s).
  4. If account_number is given: look it up in the folder's cached accounts.list, or call POST /accounts.list {FolderId, Filter:{SearchKeys:[account_number]}} ('starts with' → keep the exact SearchKey match only). No match, or Inactive=true → 400. Never create accounts implicitly.
  5. If force_merge is set: GET /companies.info?Id={force_merge} and check Data.Folder.Id = FolderId (D13), or POST /companies.list {FolderId, Filter:{Id: force_merge}} (folder-scoped by its FolderId). Reject with 400 if it returns 404/empty, if Folder.Id ≠ FolderId, or if IsSupplier = false. If IsCustomer is already true, skip the update. Otherwise POST /companies.update (Id-only call, sent only after the folder check) {CompanyId: force_merge, IsCustomer: true, CustomerConcentrationAccount (if given), ExternalReference (if given and policy allows)}, with every other field null/omitted and never "" ("" erases data). Then POST /ibans.list {CompanyId}, and POST /ibans.new {FolderId, CompanyId, Iban} for each Chift IBAN not already present (omit IsDefault so the supplier's main IBAN is kept). Set Id = force_merge and go to step 7.
  6. Duplicate pre-check (normal path). If vat: POST /companies.list {FolderId, Filter:{IsVatLiable:true, VatNumber}} (equals). If external_reference: POST /companies.list {FolderId, Filter:{ExternalReference}}, keep exact matches only. Match with IsCustomer=true → 400 ERROR_PARTNER_ALREADY_EXISTS (existing_code = Horus SearchKey/Id). Supplier-only match → 400 ERROR_PARTNER_ALREADY_EXISTS whose message invites a retry with force_merge={Id}. Match that is beneficiary-only or has neither flag → 400 with the Id; never merge silently.
  7. SearchKey: derive a candidate from name (uppercase, ASCII, A-Z0-9 only, truncated; assumed rule). POST /companies.list {FolderId, Filter:{SearchKey:candidate}} and post-filter for an exact match (search keys are 'unique within the folder'). On collision, append a numeric suffix and check again.
  8. POST /companies.new {FolderId, SearchKey, Name, IsCustomer:true, IsSupplier:false, IsVatLiable, VatNumber?, AllowDuplicateVatNumber:false, LanguageCode, CountryCode, Address?, ZipCode?, Locality?, PhoneNumber?, Website?, ContactEmail?, InvoiceEmail?, ExternalReference?, CustomerConcentrationAccount?, CustomerActivitySuspended?, Ibans?:[{Iban, IsDefault:true, IsDomiciliation:false}]} → Id. Error handling: 400 → do not retry as-is. Log ErrorType/Details. Regenerate the SearchKey once only if ErrorType/Details points to a SearchKey conflict (ErrorType values undocumented). A duplicate VAT → 400 ERROR_PARTNER_ALREADY_EXISTS. Anything else → Chift 400 with Details. 401 → refresh once and replay (the token was rejected, so the create was not processed, D); if the refresh fails, mark the connection disconnected. 403 → Chift 400 folder not accessible. 500, timeout or network error → do NOT apply the generic exponential-backoff retry blindly. First search companies.list by ExternalReference (exact), then VatNumber (equals), then the generated SearchKey (exact), and re-POST only if nothing is found.
  9. Read-back. Documented path: GET /companies.info?Id={Id}, then check Data.Folder.Id = FolderId (D13). A mismatch should never happen because FolderId was sent on create; if it does, return 400 with the created Id in the error detail, log it, and never re-POST. The response gives all scalar fields, LastModifiedDate, SearchKey, MainIban, Ibans refs and the CustomerConcentrationAccount ref. Alternative to test (assumption): POST /companies.list {FolderId, Filter:{Id}, Include:"MainIban,Ibans,CustomerConcentrationAccount"} (folder-scoped by construction), resolving the refs from Included by Type then Id. That would replace steps 7-9 with one call.
  10. POST /ibans.list {CompanyId: Id} → bank_accounts[] (is_primary = Data[].Id == MainIban.Id). ibans.list has no FolderId and returns no Folder, so its folder guard is the step-7 check on the company (D13). Skip when companies.info Data.Ibans is empty.
  11. If Data.CustomerConcentrationAccount is not null: resolve its Id through the cached, folder-scoped accounts.list (Data[].Id → SearchKey), or call GET /accounts.info?Id=… and check Data.Folder.Id = FolderId (D13, else 400) → account_number. Expect a value even when account_number was omitted, because Horus applies a default (derived).
  12. Build ClientItemOut from steps 7-9 with the shared Horus company → Chift partner mapper (D9): active = NOT CustomerActivitySuspended (assumption), true when absent; one 'main' address from Address (+Address2), ZipCode, Locality, CountryCode, with address-level phone/email = null; phone = ContactPhone; email = ContactEmail; is_company = true; language = lowercase Horus Language, 'XX' → null; third_party_account = company SearchKey; account_number = CustomerConcentrationAccount resolved to its SearchKey. The response is mapped from the read-back, not from the request. If companies.new succeeded but the read-back keeps failing, return at least id plus the echoed request values and log the failure. Never re-POST.

Invoice creation (multiple analytic plans) Supported with workaround

POST /consumers/{consumer_id}/accounting/invoices/multi-analytic-plans

Customer invoices and refunds go to invoices.sales.new, supplier invoices and refunds to invoices.purchases.new, with Type 0 for an invoice and 1 for a credit note. The PDF can be sent in the same call (multipart). The connector first resolves the daybook, the partner, account search keys and cost sections from cached reference data, translates each Chift tax_code into the Horus VAT fields, converts analytic percentages into amounts, checks for an existing document number, and reads the entry back after creation.

Worked example: analytic distribution

Chift line: base amount 1,000.00; plan DEPARTMENT split 50 % SALES / 50 % SUPPORT; plan PROJECT 100 % ALPHA.

Horus cost lines (amounts on combinations of cost sections, summing to the line base amount):

AmountDEPARTMENTPROJECT
500.00SALESALPHA
500.00SUPPORTALPHA

Combinations are the product of the plan percentages. Rounding uses the largest remainder so the cost lines always sum exactly to the base amount. Deriving combinations from independent plan splits is an assumption Chift should validate (question C9).

VAT: from Chift tax_code to Horus fields

The connector exposes Horus tax combinations as synthetic VAT codes, for example S-21-0-0 = sale, 21 %, origin 0 (national sale), type 0 (national). On write the code is decoded into TaxRate, TaxOrigin and TaxType (plus TaxKind, TaxCondition and TaxConditionValue for purchases). The list of valid combinations comes from Horus reference spreadsheets that still need to be obtained (question H1).

referencePOST /invoices.*.new → Comment (shared with customer_memo)CompositeHorus has no dedicated field for an external or supplier invoice number. Header Comment convention (D3): Comment = '{reference}' when only reference is set; '{reference} | {customer_memo}' when both are set; '| {customer_memo}' when only customer_memo is set (the leading separator means 'no reference'); null when neither is set. Separator = space, pipe, space. Read routes split on the first ' | ' (left → reference, right → customer_memo) and must also treat a Comment starting with '| ' as reference = null. A reference that itself contains ' | ' would split wrongly on read, so the connector replaces or rejects it (connector policy). The reference is lost as a structured, filterable field. Comment maximum length is undocumented (to confirm).DerivedPOST /invoices.purchases.new header free-text fields are only "PaymentReferences: string|null" and "Comment: string|null"; Guide: Posting sales & purchase invoices example: "Comment": "Invoice 2024-09-001"; convention: decision D3

payment_method_idPOST /invoices.*.new → PaymentModeTransformThe PaymentMode enum and its labels are documented (documented). Convention, to validate with Chift (assumption): the Chift payment method id is the Horus PaymentMode value as a string ('0','100','110','200','400','410','420','900','910'), converted to a number, and Chift GET payment-methods is emulated as a static list of these labels. null → omit (NULL default). lines[].untaxed_amount req.POST /invoices.*.new → InvoiceLines[].BaseAmountDirectBaseAmount = untaxed_amount (absolute value for refunds, assumption). It is also the base for the CostLines amounts.DocumentedPOST /invoices.sales.new → InvoiceLines[] "BaseAmount *: number [example=1000]"
lines[].tax_rate req.POST /invoices.*.new → InvoiceLines[].TaxRateDirectSame unit (percent: 21 = 21%). Must match the rate encoded in tax_code, otherwise 422. lines[].tax_amount req.POST /invoices.*.new → InvoiceLines[].TaxAmountDirectTaxAmount = tax_amount. Send OverrideTaxAmounts=true so Horus keeps the issued invoice's exact VAT instead of recomputing base × rate.DocumentedPOST /invoices.sales.new → "OverrideTaxAmounts ... By default, all tax amounts are recalculated (base amount X tax rate) and adapted if the provided tax amount is within 1 cent of the calculated one."; Guide: Posting sales & purchase invoices: "Set to true to force your own tax amounts."
lines[].total req.noneChift-sideValidation only (total = untaxed_amount + tax_amount). Horus lines have no total.DerivedPOST /invoices.sales.new → InvoiceLines[] has BaseAmount and TaxAmount only
lines[].account_number req.POST /invoices.*.new → InvoiceLines[].AccountDirectAccount = account_number (the Horus account search key). Pre-validate against the cached accounts.list: exact SearchKey match and Inactive=false. Optionally reject IsSummary=true or collective accounts (e.g. IntCategory COLLECCUS), since Chift requires a general account (assumption: Horus posting behaviour is undocumented).DocumentedPOST /invoices.sales.new → "Account *: string [example="700000"] // The account's search key"; Guide: Data model: "When posting entries, you reference accounts by their search key."; POST /accounts.list → IsSummary, IntCategory [example="COLLECCUS"]
lines[].tax_code req.POST /invoices.*.new → InvoiceLines[].TaxOrigin + TaxType (+ TaxKind/TaxCondition/TaxConditionValue for purchases, + TaxCountryCode for sales)TransformHorus has no VAT-code Id. The connector owns a VAT catalogue built from the Horus accepted-combination files (not provided; requested from Horus) and exposes it through Chift GET vat-codes with ONE canonical id grammar (D2), used identically for decoding here and for encoding in Invoice retrieval. Sales: 'S-{TaxRate}-{TaxOrigin}-{TaxType}', plus '-{TaxCountryCode}' only for OSS lines (TaxOrigin = 13); example 'S-21-0-0'. Purchases: 'P-{TaxRate}-{TaxOrigin}-{TaxType}-{TaxKind}-{TaxCondition}-{TaxConditionValue}', with 'x' for an unused component; example 'P-21-2-0-0-1-true'. Decoding: the id must exist in the catalogue (otherwise 422) and carry the prefix of the invoice side (S- for customer_*, P- for supplier_*); its components fill TaxRate/TaxOrigin/TaxType (+ TaxKind/TaxCondition/TaxConditionValue for purchases, null for 'x'; + TaxCountryCode for OSS sales). The Chift scope nat/eu/int published in GET vat-codes follows TaxType 0/1/2. Horus VatCode is never accepted or returned as the Chift tax_code (D2). Optional (assumption): human labels for the catalogue entries (labels only, never ids) by mining the distinct VatDetail combinations already used in the folder (account-histories.list).DerivedPOST /invoices.sales.new → "TaxOrigin *: number enum[0..20]"; "TaxType *: number enum[0, 1, 2] // - 0: National - 1: European - 2: International"; POST /invoices.purchases.new → TaxKind "0: MERCH (Merchandises)", TaxCondition "1: DEDUC (Deductible)", TaxConditionValue "true: DEDUC or INVEST"; Guide: Posting sales & purchase invoices: "Only some combinations are valid. The accepted combinations are listed in these reference files"; id grammar: decision D2
lines[].descriptionPOST /invoices.*.new → InvoiceLines[].CommentDirectComment = description. Omit when empty. Maximum length undocumented.DocumentedPOST /invoices.sales.new → InvoiceLines[] "Comment: string [example="My comment for the line"]"
lines[].analytic_distributionPOST /invoices.*.new → InvoiceLines[].CostLines[]CompositeConvert the per-plan percentage splits into Horus cost lines: an amount plus one Costs entry per schema, with up to 8 per cost line. One plan: one CostLine per analytic account. Several plans: Cartesian product of the accounts, weight = product of the percentages (independence assumption); a plan at 100% on one account is added to every row. The sum of CostLines.Amount must equal BaseAmount, which the D8 largest-remainder rounding guarantees. Maximum 8 schemas. Leave CostDistributionKey null (cost lines take precedence anyway). Plans and sections are resolved only from the per-folder D8 cache. Reject (422) a distribution when the folder has WithAnalytics=false, or when a plan is a Free schema (CostSectionType 900), which is excluded from Chift analytics in v1 (D8).DerivedPOST /invoices.sales.new → CostLines[] "Amount *: ... The sum of all cost lines' amounts must be equal to the corresponding invoice line's base amount."; POST /cost-histories.list: "A folder can use up to 8 different cost schemas." "Every cost history can contain up to 8 cost sections."; Guide: Posting sales & purchase invoices: "If both are provided, cost lines take precedence."
lines[].analytic_distribution[].analytic_plan req.POST /invoices.*.new → CostLines[].Costs[].SchemaSearchKey (from POST /cost-schemas.list)LookupHorus posting needs the cost schema search key (documented). Id convention (D8): the Chift analytic_plan is the Horus cost schema GUID Id (stable across renames, and the id Get analytic plans returns), resolved to SearchKey through the per-folder D8 cache (cost-schemas.list), never through a per-call cost-schemas.info. An id that is not in the folder's cache → 422, which also blocks ids from another folder (D13). A Free schema (CostSectionType 900) → 422 (excluded in v1, D8).DerivedPOST /invoices.sales.new → "SchemaSearchKey *: string [example="DEPARTMENT"] // The search key of an existing cost schema."; POST /cost-schemas.list → Data[].Id, SearchKey, CostSectionType; Guide: Data model: "Accounts and cost schemas/sections are referenced by search key in posting endpoints."
lines[].analytic_distribution[].analytic_accounts[] req.POST /invoices.*.new → CostLines[] rowsCompositeEach account in a plan becomes one Costs entry {SchemaSearchKey, Value} inside every CostLine row of the product (see analytic_distribution).DerivedPOST /invoices.sales.new → CostLines[].Costs[] {SchemaSearchKey *, Value *}
lines[].analytic_distribution[].analytic_accounts[].analytic_account req.POST /invoices.*.new → CostLines[].Costs[].Value (from POST /cost-sections.list)LookupId convention (D8): the Chift analytic_account is the cost section GUID Id, resolved to its SearchKey through the D8 cache (cost-sections.list per List-type schema with Filter.IncludeInactive = true), never through a per-call cost-sections.info. The section must belong to the plan's schema (CostSchema.Id), otherwise 422. An inactive section (Inactive = true) → 422 'analytic account inactive' (D8). IsLocked behaviour is undocumented (to confirm). Free schemas are not accepted on write in v1 (D8).DerivedPOST /invoices.sales.new → "Value *: string [example="OFFICE"] // The search key of an existing cost section, linked to this cost schema"; POST /cost-sections.list → IncludeInactive "*FALSE* by default", Data[].Inactive, IsLocked, CostSchema {Type: "CostSchemas", Id}
lines[].analytic_distribution[].analytic_accounts[].percentage req.POST /invoices.*.new → CostLines[].AmountTransformEach CostLine row amount = BaseAmount × product(pct/100) over the plans of that row. Rounding (D8): largest-remainder method on the per-line product allocation: floor every row to the cent, then give the remaining cents one by one to the rows with the largest fractional remainders, so the cost lines sum exactly to the line BaseAmount. Example: 0.10 split 33/33/34 gives 0.03/0.03/0.04.DerivedPOST /invoices.sales.new → CostLines[] "Amount *: number [example=1000] // The sum of all cost lines' amounts must be equal to the corresponding invoice line's base amount."

Call sequence

  1. Connection context (Chift-internal). consumer_id → the stored Horus connection: one per license, holding api_url, access token, refresh token and expiry. Access tokens last 1 hour. Refresh proactively a few minutes before expiry, under a per-connection lock, and persist the new token pair before using it, because refresh tokens are single-use (Guide: Token lifecycle). Send Content-Encoding: gzip (Guide: Making requests) and ignore unknown response properties (Guide: Changes & versioning). FolderId (D7): folder_id when given; if it is null and exactly one folder was selected at activation, that folder; if no selection is stored, the single folder returned by POST /folders.list {Mode: 0}; otherwise 400 'folder_id required'.
  2. Chift-side validation, before any Horus call: - total = untaxed_amount + tax_amount on the header and on each line. - Header totals = sum of the lines; otherwise invoice_correction is needed. - Analytic percentages are integers summing to 100 per plan, with at most 8 plans. - Every tax_code (and correction tax code) is a D2 catalogue id with the prefix of the invoice side (S- for customer_*, P- for supplier_*). - The PDF type is one of Pdf/Xml/CODA/SODA/jpg/jpeg/png. - Reject unsupported inputs with 400/422: withholding_tax, nl_payment_terms_split, a force_financial_period that differs from the invoice_date month, a currency outside the Horus enum, a non-numeric invoice_number.
  3. If regroup_lines='true' (the default), merge lines that share (account_number, tax_code, analytic_distribution), summing untaxed_amount, tax_amount and total.
  4. Reference data, loaded once and cached per folder (Guide: Reading accounting data): - POST /daybooks.list: resolve DaybookId with the D1 filter (customer_invoice [200]; customer_refund [250], else [200]; supplier_invoice [100]; supplier_refund [150], else [100]); exactly one candidate, otherwise 400 'journal_id required'. A given journal_id must be in this folder-scoped list with a Type of the invoice side. - POST /accounts.list: exact search key, Inactive=false, optionally IsSummary=false. - Analytics cache (D8): POST /cost-schemas.list, then POST /cost-sections.list per List-type schema with Filter.IncludeInactive = true. Map GUID ids to search keys; read Mandatory and CostSectionType. - POST /fiscal-years.list, then GET /fiscal-years.info: fiscal year bounds, closure dates and IsClosed for invoice_date. Horus recommends this closure check (Guide: Data model). - GET /folders.info: WithAnalytics, Language, CountryCode.
  5. Partner (Guide: Managing customers & suppliers: 'find or create the company first'). - partner_id set: CompanyId = partner_id. Guard with GET /companies.info (cached): Data.Folder.Id must equal folder_id, otherwise 400 (D13); check IsCustomer/IsSupplier for the invoice side. - partner_id null and partner_info set (D10): SearchKey = account_number sanitised with Client creation's rules. POST /companies.list (FolderId, Filter.SearchKey), then post-filter on an exact match in the same folder. If the match lacks IsCustomer (customer_*) or IsSupplier (supplier_*), POST /companies.update with CompanyId + that flag only (never "" values). If no match, POST /companies.new with Client creation's required-field rules (LanguageCode = folder Language per D9, CountryCode = folder CountryCode, IsVatLiable=false, optional ExternalReference), and use the returned Id as CompanyId. - Both null: reject, because CompanyId is required.
  6. VAT (D2): decode each lines[].tax_code, and the correction tax code, through the connector's catalogue: - Sales 'S-{TaxRate}-{TaxOrigin}-{TaxType}' → TaxRate, TaxOrigin, TaxType; OSS ids (TaxOrigin=13) carry a fifth component → TaxCountryCode. - Purchases 'P-{TaxRate}-{TaxOrigin}-{TaxType}-{TaxKind}-{TaxCondition}-{TaxConditionValue}' → the six fields, 'x' → null. An id not in the catalogue → 422. Check the decoded rate against lines[].tax_rate.
  7. Numbering and duplicate pre-check (D12). Number = int(invoice_number). - invoice_number set: POST /book-entries.list (FolderId, DaybookId, fiscal-year months, Filter.DocumentNo=Number) before creating. Unconfirmed entries are included by default. If an entry exists, return 409 Conflict (or the existing entry, per Chift policy). This follows Horus's advice to reconcile on daybook + entry number. - invoice_number null: a draft gets Number=0. A posted sale gets max(DocumentNo)+1 from the same call (assumption, race risk) or is rejected. A posted purchase gets null (nullable).
  8. Build the Horus body. - Header: Type, Number, Date, DueDate, CompanyId, totals, PaymentReferences, PaymentMode, CurrencyCode/CurrencyRate, OverrideTaxAmounts=true, AllowUnbalanced=false. - Comment (D3): '{reference}', '{reference} | {customer_memo}' or '| {customer_memo}'. - Confirmed (D12): false for draft; true for posted unless the per-consumer 'always push unconfirmed' setting is on. - InvoiceLines with CostLines: percentages become amounts as a product of combinations across plans, rounded with the largest-remainder method so the cost lines sum exactly to BaseAmount (D8). - Append an invoice_correction line when header and lines differ.
  9. Create: POST /invoices.sales.new (customer_*) or POST /invoices.purchases.new (supplier_*). Without a PDF, use application/json. With a PDF, use multipart/form-data: file = the base64-decoded bytes, body = the JSON as a plain text form field, including FileName with its extension. Error handling (Guide: Responses & errors): - 400: never retry as-is. Map to Chift 400/422 and surface ErrorType and Details. - 404: map to Chift 404. - 401: refresh once under the lock and retry. If the refresh fails, mark the connection disconnected. - 403: map to Chift 400 'folder not accessible with this connection' (D7). - 500 or timeout: before any retry, re-run the step-6 DocumentNo pre-check (D12), because Horus has no idempotency key and no invoice delete. Then retry with exponential backoff. Never auto-retry a Number=0 draft.
  10. If ignore_accounting_id=true: return the echoed request plus id = the Horus Id right away. Horus is synchronous, so the Id is already known.
  11. Otherwise, read back with GET /book-entries.info?Id=<Id>: one call by Id, with no FolderId, DaybookId or period. Check Data.Folder.Id = folder_id (D13). It gives DocumentNo, Confirmed, Date, LastModifiedDate (last_updated_on), CompanyDetail (Company, DueDate, TotalAmount, TurnoverAmount, TaxAmount, RemarkPayment), Daybook and Document. Its CompanyDetail amounts are assumed EUR (D4) and are never returned under a foreign currency code. Add POST /account-histories.list (FolderId, the fiscal-year period, Filter.BookEntryId=Id, IncludeVatDetail, IncludeCostDetail, IncludeCompanyDetail, optionally Include='CompanyDetail.Company') only when a field needs line data: the EUR partner-line amount and applied CurrencyRate of a foreign-currency invoice, the partner collective account, or booked VAT/cost detail. If that list returns nothing, fall back to GET /account-histories.info for each AccountHistories[].Id from book-entries.info. Optionally call GET /documents.info with Document.Id for the file name. If the read-back fails, never re-create the invoice: return the Id with echoed values.
  12. Assemble InvoiceItemOutMultiAnalyticPlans from the request echo, the resolved Ids and the read-back values (see the response mappings). Currency rule (D4): amounts are the echoed invoice-currency amounts with the echoed currency; accounting_info.main_currency_total = the EUR partner-line amount; EUR amounts are never paired with a foreign currency code.

Outstanding items (customers and suppliers) Supported with workaround

GET /consumers/{consumer_id}/accounting/outstandings

Horus has folder-level open-item lists for both sides, but they differ. For clients, invoices.sales.outstanding.list gives open sales invoices with their open amount, joined with customers.outstanding for the account and matching details. For suppliers, only ledger lines are available (suppliers.outstanding), so the open amount is computed from matching groups. Pagination is emulated, and amounts are signed per side (positive means owed).

Sign convention

Customer lines: amount = debit − credit. Supplier lines: amount = credit − debit. A positive amount is always owed (a receivable for clients, a payable for suppliers). Chift’s expected convention is question C5.

items[].open_amount req.client: Data[].OpenAmount / OpenCurrencyAmount; supplier (and client reconciliation/fallback lines): computed from AccountHistories[].Debit, Credit, MatchDetailCompositeClient: OpenCurrencyAmount in the foreign view, else OpenAmount (native, receivable view, D4). items[].reference(1) partner-line AccountHistories[].Comment (suppliers.outstanding; for clients the customers.outstanding line joined on AccountHistory.Id) → (2) GET /book-entries.info → Data.CompanyDetail.RemarkCompany → (3) client only: POST /invoices.sales.outstanding.list → Data[].RemarkCompositeShared header-comment convention (D3). Source chain, first non-empty value wins (assumption: where the header Comment surfaces on read is undocumented). (1) The partner-line Comment. (2) CompanyDetail.RemarkCompany of the line's book entry, via GET /book-entries.info (step 4c(v)), used only if CompanyDetail.Company.Id = partner_id. (3) For client sales-list rows only, Data[].Remark. If the optional book-entry enrichment is switched off, the chain is (1) → (3). For lines in an invoice daybook (Type 100/150/200/250, where Invoice creation writes the convention), split the value on the first ' | '. The left part becomes reference (null if empty, e.g. '| memo'). The right part is customer_memo, which OutstandingItem does not have, so it is dropped. Other lines (payments, miscellaneous operations) keep the value as is. Empty -> null.AssumptionPOST /customers.outstanding | /suppliers.outstanding → AccountHistories[].Comment: string|null; GET /book-entries.info → Data.CompanyDetail.RemarkCompany: string|null (no description); POST /invoices.sales.outstanding.list → Data[].Remark: string|null [example="My invoice remark"]; POST /invoices.sales.new / invoices.purchases.new → Comment: string|null [example="my global comment"]; Guide: Posting sales & purchase invoices example: '"Comment": "Invoice 2024-09-001"'
items[].matching_numbersAccountHistories[].MatchDetail.MatchNo (client: via the customers.outstanding join or sideload)Transform[String(MatchNo)] if MatchNo is not null, otherwise []. MatchNo is scoped per company (customer/supplier) or per general account, so it is unique only together with partner_id (derived). If MatchStatus is set but MatchNo is null, treat the line as unmatched.DerivedMatchDetail.MatchNo: number|null; Guide: Outstanding items & matching: 'for customers and suppliers ( 100 / 200 ), all lines must belong to the same company'; GET /matching.next-number.customer: 'Get the next available "MatchNo" for a given customer.'; customers.outstanding example: 'MatchStatus': 0, 'MatchNo': null

Use it only if the entry's CompanyDetail.Company.Id equals the line's partner_id. book-entries.info is the default (1 call per distinct entry, no daybook or period, cacheable by BookEntry.Id); book-entries.list per daybook/period is the bulk alternative for a cold cache (step 4c).
